# Codebase Ugliness Cleanup — Full Execution Plan

## Context

Deep dive audit identified 6 themes of technical debt (~40 individual items) across the Reigh frontend and edge functions. Detailed plans exist in `tasks/2026-02-13-P1-through-P6*.md`. This plan covers **how to execute all of them** using parallel sub-agents with zero file conflicts between concurrent agents.

The key constraint: **ProjectContext.tsx** is touched by 4 different plans (P1, P2, P4, P5), making it the serial bottleneck. Everything else parallelizes cleanly.

## Execution: 5 Waves

### Wave 1 — Quick Wins (8 parallel agents, all independent files)

| Agent | Task | Files Modified |
|-------|------|----------------|
| **A** | Delete dead hooks: `useToolPageHeader`, `useGenerationTaskPreloader`, `useEnhancedGenerations`, `invalidateGenerationUpdate` + update `data_fetching.md` | `ToolPageHeaderContext.tsx` (56L), `GenerationTaskContext.tsx` (122L), `useGenerationInvalidation.ts` (314L), `data_fetching.md` |
| **B** | Remove useUpscale 5 dead fields + update consumers | `useUpscale.ts` (133L), `InlineEditView.tsx` (956L), find+update BottomLeftControls |
| **C** | Remove `promptsByShot`/`masterPromptByShot` type fields + fix misleading DEPRECATED comments | `image-generation/settings.ts` (97L), `ImageGenerationForm/types.ts` (362L), `travel-between-images/settings.ts` (120L) |
| **D** | Replace hardcoded colors with theme tokens | `OnboardingModal.tsx` (618L), `DatasetBrowserModal.tsx` (651L) |
| **E** | Fix imagesPerPrompt silent change — store per-mode values | `ImageGenerationForm.tsx` (195L) |
| **F** | Unify LoRA types + extract `buildHiresFixParams` + migrate imageInpaint to shared `processBatchResults` | `shared/types/lora.ts` (NEW), `annotatedImageEdit.ts` (123L), `imageInpaint.ts` (122L), `imageGeneration.ts` (504L), `magicEdit.ts` (331L), `zImageTurboI2I.ts` (249L) |
| **G** | Merge complete_task double query into single query (add FK migration if needed) | `complete_task/index.ts` (371L), possibly new migration |
| **H** | ReferenceSectionProps: children consume context directly instead of 35 drilled props | `reference/types.ts` (96L), `ReferenceSection.tsx` (153L), child components |

**After Wave 1:** `git add . && git commit` checkpoint

### Wave 2 — Consistency & Mobile Basics (5 parallel agents)

| Agent | Task | Files Modified |
|-------|------|----------------|
| **I** | Consolidate `useDeviceDetection.ts` into `use-mobile.tsx` — single source of truth | `use-mobile.tsx` (103L), `useDeviceDetection.ts` (174L) |
| **J** | Unify PanesContext 3 identical lock setters into factory function | `PanesContext.tsx` (283L) |
| **K** | PaymentSuccessPage: replace 4 overlapping timeouts with state machine | `PaymentSuccessPage.tsx` (181L) |
| **L** | Remove `@ts-nocheck` from 7 edge functions (add minimal types) | `ai-prompt`, `ai-voice-prompt`, `broadcast-realtime`, `generate-pat`, `revoke-pat`, `stripe-webhook`, `tasks-list` |
| **M** | Add task status transition guards (Complete → Queued/InProgress blocked) | `update-task-status/index.ts` (603L) |

**After Wave 2:** `git add . && git commit` checkpoint

### Wave 3 — Mobile Architecture + Backend (5 agents, N before R on ProjectContext)

| Agent | Task | Files Modified |
|-------|------|----------------|
| **N** | Extract `useMobileTimeoutFallback` hook, refactor 3 contexts to use it, replace inline UA sniffing | `ProjectContext.tsx` (653L), `UserSettingsContext.tsx` (179L), `AuthContext.tsx` (143L), NEW: `useMobileTimeoutFallback.ts` |
| **O** | Standardize edge function auth to use `authenticateRequest()` | ~10 edge functions with manual JWT pattern |
| **P** | Fix ai-prompt rate limiting: `clientIp` → `user.id` | `ai-prompt/index.ts` (368L) |
| **Q** | Extract shared `_shared/orchestrator.ts`, deduplicate billing logic in 3 functions | NEW: `_shared/orchestrator.ts`, `calculate-task-cost/index.ts` (469L), `update-task-status/index.ts` (603L) |
| **R** | Clean window globals: type properly, gate behind DEV, remove `__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` | `browser-extensions.d.ts`, `client.ts`, `TasksPane.tsx`, `mobileProjectDebug.ts` (skip ProjectContext — handled by N) |

**N and R both touch ProjectContext** — N runs first (mobile timeout extraction), R skips ProjectContext (its global cleanup deferred to Wave 4 Agent S).

**After Wave 3:** `git add . && git commit` checkpoint, deploy changed edge functions

### Wave 4 — Structural Refactors (3 parallel agents)

| Agent | Task | Files Modified |
|-------|------|----------------|
| **S** | Decompose ProjectContext 653L → ~5 hooks + thin provider. Also clean `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` global. | `ProjectContext.tsx`, NEW: `useProjectCRUD.ts`, `useProjectSelection.ts`, `useProjectDefaults.ts`, `useProjectSettings.ts` |
| **T** | App.tsx: add AuthGate component, reorder/group providers | `App.tsx` (272L), NEW: `AuthGate.tsx` |
| **U** | Edge functions final pass: add SystemLogger + standardize CORS in remaining functions | Remaining ~15 edge functions |

**After Wave 4:** `git add . && git commit` checkpoint, deploy edge functions

### Wave 5 — Monolith Decomposition (3 at a time, priority order)

**Wave 5a:**
| Agent | Target | Strategy |
|-------|--------|----------|
| **V** | ImageEditContext (319L, 70+ fields) | Split into EditModeContext, BrushToolContext, CanvasContext, EditFormContext |
| **W** | SegmentSettingsForm (1044L, 47 props) | Extract AdvancedSettingsSection, StructureVideoSection, PromptField |
| **X** | MediaGallery (752L, 119 props) | Create GalleryConfig object, extract LightboxCoordinator, add MediaGalleryContext |

**Wave 5b:**
| Agent | Target | Strategy |
|-------|--------|----------|
| **Y** | ImageLightbox (1030L, 45 props) | Extract ImageEditOrchestrator, mode-specific panels |
| **Z** | TimelineContainer (988L) | Extract useTimelineInteractions, useTimelineZoom, useTimelineSelection |
| **AA** | useGenerationActions (727L, 14 refs) | Split into useImageAdd, useImageRemove, useImagePosition |

**Wave 5c:** VideoLightbox, InlineEditView, EditModePanel, useAutoSaveSettings, useTrainingData

## Verification

After each wave:
1. `npm run build` — no TypeScript errors
2. `grep -r` for removed identifiers — confirm no dangling references
3. Manual smoke test of affected UI areas
4. Git commit as checkpoint

After all waves:
- Full app smoke test (project creation, image generation, travel tool, edit tools)
- Edge function deployment + test (complete_task, ai-prompt, update-task-status)

<result>TypeScript compiles clean with no errors.

Now let me verify the three scenarios from the task:

**Verification 1: Switching automated -> managed saves the automated value and restores managed value.**

Looking at lines 74-87: When switching from automated to managed:
- `outgoingMode` = `'automated'` (the current mode before the switch)
- Line 77: `automatedImagesPerPromptRef.current = form.imagesPerPrompt` -- saves current value (e.g., 4) to the automated slot
- Line 81: `form.setEffectivePromptMode('managed')` -- switches mode
- Line 86: `form.setImagesPerPrompt(managedImagesPerPromptRef.current)` -- restores the managed value (default 1, or whatever the user had set previously)

Correct.

**Verification 2: Switching managed -> automated saves the managed value and restores automated value.**

When switching from managed to automated:
- `outgoingMode` = `'managed'`
- Line 79: `managedImagesPerPromptRef.current = form.imagesPerPrompt` -- saves current value to the managed slot
- Line 81: `form.setEffectivePromptMode('automated')` -- switches mode
- Line 84: `form.setImagesPerPrompt(automatedImagesPerPromptRef.current)` -- restores the automated value (default 8, or whatever the user had set previously)

Correct.

**Verification 3: First-time switching still defaults to 8 (automated) and 1 (managed).**

The refs are initialized on line 61-62:
- `automatedImagesPerPromptRef = useRef(8)` -- automated default is 8
- `managedImagesPerPromptRef = useRef(1)` -- managed default is 1

On first mount, the form starts in automated mode with `imagesPerPrompt = 8`. When the user switches to managed for the first time:
- The automated slot saves the current value (8)
- The managed slot returns its initial value (1)

When switching back to automated:
- The managed slot saves the current value (1 or whatever they changed it to)
- The automated slot returns 8 (or whatever they had changed it to)

Correct.

   ```tsx
   const managedImagesPerPromptRef = useRef(1);
   ```

3. **Lines 72-88** -- Replaced the mode-switching handler. Instead of blindly resetting to hardcoded defaults (8 for automated, 1 for managed), it now:
   - Saves the current `imagesPerPrompt` to the outgoing mode's ref slot before switching
   - Restores `imagesPerPrompt` from the incoming mode's ref slot after switching
   
   ```tsx
   onPromptModeChange={(mode) => {
     form.markAsInteracted();
     // Save current imagesPerPrompt to the outgoing mode's slot
     const outgoingMode = form.effectivePromptMode;
     if (outgoingMode === 'automated') {
       automatedImagesPerPromptRef.current = form.imagesPerPrompt;
     } else if (outgoingMode === 'managed') {
       managedImagesPerPromptRef.current = form.imagesPerPrompt;
     }
     form.setEffectivePromptMode(mode);
     // Restore imagesPerPrompt from the incoming mode's slot
     if (mode === 'automated') {
       form.setImagesPerPrompt(automatedImagesPerPromptRef.current);
     } else if (mode === 'managed') {
       form.setImagesPerPrompt(managedImagesPerPromptRef.current);
     }
   }}
   ```

Using refs (not state) avoids extra re-renders since these values are just "memory" -- they only need to be read at the moment of switching. The defaults (8 for automated, 1 for managed) match the previous hardcoded behavior for first-time use.</result>

<result>Everything is in order. Here is the summary of changes:

---

## Summary

### Problem
`fetchTaskContext` in `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts` made two sequential database queries: one to fetch the task, then a second to fetch task type metadata from `task_types` by matching on the `name` column. A comment said "no FK relationship exists" to justify the separate query.

### Changes Made

#### 1. New Migration: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/migrations/20260213000000_add_tasks_task_type_fkey.sql`

Creates the foreign key constraint from `tasks.task_type` to `task_types.name`:

```sql
ALTER TABLE tasks
  ADD CONSTRAINT tasks_task_type_fkey
  FOREIGN KEY (task_type) REFERENCES task_types(name);
```

This is safe because:
- `tasks.task_type` is `text NOT NULL`
- `task_types.name` is `text NOT NULL UNIQUE`
- All existing SQL in migrations already joins these tables on `t.task_type = tt.name`

#### 2. Updated `fetchTaskContext` in `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts`

Merged the two sequential queries into a single query using PostgREST's FK join syntax:

```ts
const { data: task, error } = await supabase
  .from("tasks")
  .select(`id, task_type, project_id, params, task_types!tasks_task_type_fkey(tool_type, category, content_type, variant_type)`)
  .eq("id", taskId)
  .single();
```

The joined `task_types` data comes back as `task.task_types` (an object), which is then extracted into the same `TaskContext` shape as before. The `TaskContext` interface is unchanged, so no downstream code needs modification -- `generation.ts`, `generation-handlers.ts`, `generation-child.ts`, and `orchestrator.ts` all continue to work identically.

### What did NOT change
- The `TaskContext` interface (lines 27-36)
- Any downstream consumer of `TaskContext`
- Any logic in the completion flow
- Error handling behavior (still returns `null` on error/not-found)
- The fallback defaults (`'unknown'`, `'image'`, `null`) for missing task type metadata</result>

Here is a summary of what was done in `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/PanesContext.tsx`:

**Problem:** Three nearly-identical functions (`setIsGenerationsPaneLocked`, `setIsShotsPaneLocked`, `setIsTasksPaneLocked`) each duplicated the same lock-toggle logic -- exclusive locking on mobile/tablet, skipping persistence on small phones, and standard toggle on desktop. This was ~60 lines of repetitive code.

**Solution:** Replaced them with a `createPaneLockSetter` factory function (lines 115-141) that takes a `lockKey` (`'gens' | 'shots' | 'tasks'`) and returns a setter function with the shared logic:

```ts
const createPaneLockSetter = useCallback(
  (lockKey: 'gens' | 'shots' | 'tasks') => (isLocked: boolean) => {
    setLocks(prev => {
      if (prev[lockKey] === isLocked) return prev;
      const exclusiveLock = (isMobile || isTablet) && isLocked;
      const newLocks = exclusiveLock
        ? { shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }
        : { ...prev, [lockKey]: isLocked };
      if (!isSmallMobile) {
        savePaneLocks(exclusiveLock ? newLocks : { [lockKey]: isLocked });
      }
      return newLocks;
    });
    // Tasks-specific side effect preserved
    if (lockKey === 'tasks' && isLocked) {
      setIsTasksPaneOpenState(true);
    }
  },
  [savePaneLocks, isSmallMobile, isMobile, isTablet]
);

const setIsGenerationsPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('gens'), [createPaneLockSetter]);
const setIsShotsPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('shots'), [createPaneLockSetter]);
const setIsTasksPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('tasks'), [createPaneLockSetter]);
```

**Key details:**
- The tasks pane's special `setIsTasksPaneOpenState(true)` side-effect is preserved via the `lockKey === 'tasks'` guard inside the factory.
- The exclusive lock logic uses a computed spread (`{ shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }`) instead of three hand-written object literals.
- `createPaneLockSetter` is wrapped in `useCallback`; the individual setters are derived via `useMemo` so they maintain stable references.
- The `PanesContextType` interface and the `value` object are completely unchanged -- no external API changes.
- TypeScript compiles cleanly with `npx tsc --noEmit`.
- Net reduction: ~60 lines of duplicated logic replaced by ~35 lines of unified code (the file went from 283 lines to 258 lines).</result>

<summary>Agent "Wave 2K: PaymentSuccess state machine" completed</summary>
<result>Everything looks correct. Here is a summary of what was done.

---

## Summary

**File modified:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/pages/PaymentSuccessPage.tsx`

### Problems fixed

The original code had 4 overlapping timeouts that created race conditions and a dependency array bug:

1. **5000ms timeout** (line 28-32) — force success if still loading
2. **2000ms interval** (line 45) — poll credit queries
3. **30000ms timeout** (line 48-52) — cleanup everything and force success
4. **1500ms timeout** (line 60-62) — show loading briefly then force success

**Critical bug:** `verificationStatus` was in the `useEffect` dependency array (line 69). When the 1.5s timeout fired and set status to `'success'`, the entire effect re-ran, creating duplicate timers on top of old ones (though cleanup ran first, the effect immediately re-executed and set up new timeouts).

**Race condition:** Timeouts 1 (5s) and 4 (1.5s) could both try to set status to `'success'`. Timeout 3 (30s) cleared timeout 1 but also forced success, creating redundant transitions.

### Replacement: explicit state machine

```
States: 'loading' | 'polling' | 'verified' | 'timed_out'

Transitions:
  loading  -> polling:   immediately on mount
  polling  -> verified:  after 1.5s minimum display
  polling  -> timed_out: after 30s hard timeout
  verified -> (done):    polling stopped, user navigates manually
  timed_out -> (done):   polling stopped, user navigates manually
```

**Key design decisions:**

- **Single main `useEffect`** runs once on mount (no `state` in the dependency array). It sets up both timers and the poll interval, with a cleanup function that clears all three.
- **Functional state updates** (`setState(prev => ...)`) ensure transitions only happen from the expected source state. The 30s timer can only transition from `'polling'` -- if the 1.5s timer already moved to `'verified'`, the 30s timer is a no-op.
- **Separate cleanup effect** watches for terminal states (`verified` / `timed_out`) and stops the polling interval, so we don't keep invalidating queries after showing success.
- **`pollIntervalRef`** holds the interval ID in a ref so the stop-polling callback can clear it without being in the main effect's dependency array.
- **Named constants** (`MIN_LOADING_DISPLAY_MS`, `POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, `MAX_POLL_DURATION_MS`) replace the magic numbers.

### UI mapping (unchanged)

| State | Visual |
|-------|--------|
| No `session_id` | Error card with XCircle |
| `loading` / `polling` | Blue spinner card ("Processing Payment") |
| `verified` / `timed_out` | Green success card ("Payment Successful!") |

### Verification

`npx tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors.</result>

     ```ts
             : { ...prev, [lockKey]: isLocked };
           if (!isSmallMobile) savePaneLocks(exclusiveLock ? newLocks : { [lockKey]: isLocked });

         });
         if (lockKey === 'tasks' && isLocked) setIsTasksPaneOpenState(true);
       }, [savePaneLocks, isSmallMobile, isMobile, isTablet]
     );
     ```

Here is a summary of all changes made to `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/ai-prompt/index.ts`:

### 1. Rate limiting changed from IP to user ID

**Before** (lines 62-70):
```typescript
// Rate limit by IP (this is an expensive AI endpoint)
const clientIp = getClientIp(req);
const rateLimitResult = await checkRateLimit(
  supabaseAdmin,
  'ai-prompt',
  clientIp,
  RATE_LIMITS.expensive,
  '[AI-PROMPT]'
);
```

**After** (lines 61-68):
```typescript
// Rate limit by user ID (this is an expensive AI endpoint)
const rateLimitResult = await checkRateLimit(
  supabaseAdmin,
  'ai-prompt',
  user.id,
  RATE_LIMITS.expensive,
  '[AI-PROMPT]'
);
```

The `user` object is already verified at line 55-59 (auth check returns early with 401 if invalid), so `user.id` is guaranteed to be available. This matches the `RATE_LIMITS.expensive` config which already declares `identifierType: 'user'` in `_shared/rateLimit.ts`.

### 2. Removed `// @ts-nocheck` and `getClientIp` import

- Removed `// @ts-nocheck` directive from line 2
- Removed `getClientIp` from the import on line 7 (no longer used)

### 3. Typed all `any` usages with `unknown` or specific types

| Location | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `jsonResponse` param | `body: any` | `body: unknown` |
| `body` variable | `let body: any` | `let body: Record<string, unknown>` |
| Destructured fields in `generate_prompts` | `const { overallPromptText = "", ... } = body` | Explicit `String(body.x ?? "")`, `Number(body.x ?? n)`, `Array.isArray()` checks |
| Destructured fields in `edit_prompt` | `const { originalPromptText = "", ... } = body` | `String(body.x ?? "")` |
| Destructured fields in `generate_summary` | `const { promptText = "" } = body` | `String(body.promptText ?? "")` |
| Destructured fields in `enhance_segment_prompt` | `const { prompt = "", temperature = 0.7 } = body` | `String(...)`, `Number(...)` |
| `existingPrompts.map` callback | `(p: any)` | `(p: unknown)` with proper narrowing via `typeof` / `as Record<string, unknown>` |
| catch blocks | `catch (err: any)` / `err?.message` | `catch (err: unknown)` / `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` |</result>

<result>All clean. Here is a summary of what was done:

---

## Summary

### New file created

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useMobileTimeoutFallback.ts`** -- A shared hook that encapsulates the mobile stall recovery pattern. It:
- Watches an `isLoading` boolean; when it becomes `true`, starts a timer
- Uses `isMobileUA()` from `use-mobile.tsx` (instead of inline UA regex) to determine timeout duration
- Calls `onTimeout` if loading hasn't finished when the timer fires
- Automatically cleans up the timer when loading finishes or the component unmounts
- Accepts `enabled`, `mobileTimeoutMs`, and `desktopTimeoutMs` options for flexibility

### Refactored files

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx`**:
- Removed `isMobileRef` (inline `navigator.userAgent` regex)
- Removed `projectsTimeoutRef` and its cleanup useEffect
- Removed inline timeout setup from the project-fetching useEffect (lines ~515-550)
- Removed timeout clearing from `fetchProjects`'s `finally` block
- Added `useMobileTimeoutFallback({ isLoading: isLoadingProjects, onTimeout: fetchProjects, mobileTimeoutMs: 15000, desktopTimeoutMs: 10000, enabled: !!userId })`
- Removed `isLoadingProjects` from the fetch useEffect's dependency array (no longer needed since the hook handles it separately)

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/UserSettingsContext.tsx`**:
- Removed `isMobileRef` (inline `navigator.userAgent` regex)
- Removed `settingsTimeoutRef` and its cleanup useEffect
- Removed inline timeout setup from inside `fetchUserSettings` callback
- Removed timeout clearing from the `finally` block
- Removed `settingsTimeoutRef` cleanup from the user-change useEffect
- Added `handleSettingsTimeout` callback (sets empty defaults on stall)
- Added `useMobileTimeoutFallback({ isLoading: isLoadingSettings, onTimeout: handleSettingsTimeout, mobileTimeoutMs: 10000, desktopTimeoutMs: 5000, enabled: !!userId })`

### AuthContext -- intentionally unchanged

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`**: The `[AuthDebounce]` pattern is a 150ms debounce on Supabase auth state change events. It does not involve:
- Mobile UA detection
- Loading timeouts / stall recovery
- Any `navigator.userAgent` access

It is a fundamentally different pattern (event debouncing vs loading timeout fallback) and does not benefit from this shared hook. No changes were made.

### Verification
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passes with no errors
- No remaining inline `navigator.userAgent` checks in any of the three context files
- No dangling references to removed refs (`isMobileRef`, `projectsTimeoutRef`, `settingsTimeoutRef`)</result>

Here is a summary of all changes made:

## Changes Summary

### Central type declaration: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/types/browser-extensions.d.ts`

Expanded the `Window` interface to include **all** `window.__*` globals in the codebase, organized into two documented categories:

- **Structural globals** (needed at runtime for non-React access):
  - `__supabase_client__` -- Supabase client singleton, survives HMR
  - `supabase` -- shorthand alias
  - `__AUTH_MANAGER__` -- centralized auth-state listener
  - `__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` -- project context snapshot for fallback access
  - `__VIS_CHANGE_AT__` -- visibility-change timestamp (backward compat)

- **Debug-only globals** (gated behind `import.meta.env.DEV`):
  - `__REACT_QUERY_CLIENT__` -- React Query client for diagnostics
  - `__SUPABASE_WEBSOCKET_INSTANCES__` -- WebSocket instance map
  - `__projectDebugLog` -- mobile project-context debug log
  - `__DATA_FRESHNESS_MANAGER__` -- DataFreshnessManager singleton
  - `__VISIBILITY_MANAGER__` -- VisibilityManager singleton
  - `__NETWORK_STATUS_MANAGER__` -- NetworkStatusManager singleton
  - `__RECONNECT_SCHEDULER__` -- ReconnectScheduler singleton
  - `__REFACTOR_METRICS__` -- refactor metrics API

<summary>Agent "Wave 3Q: Billing logic consolidation" completed</summary>
<result>Good. `orchestrator.ts` imports `triggerCostCalculation` from `./billing.ts`, and `./billing.ts` re-exports it from `../_shared/billing.ts`. The import chain is intact.

---

Here is a summary of everything that was done.

## Summary

### Problem
Three edge functions had duplicated billing/sub-task detection logic:
1. **Sub-task detection** (checking if a task is a child of an orchestrator) -- duplicated inline logic for extracting orchestrator references from 4 param paths, UUID validation, and self-reference guarding
2. **Sub-task query filter** -- the same long `.or()` PostgREST filter string was duplicated 5 times across `calculate-task-cost` and `update-task-status`
3. **Cost calculation trigger** -- the same HTTP call to `calculate-task-cost` was implemented independently in `complete_task/billing.ts` and `update-task-status/index.ts`

### Solution

Created `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts` with four shared exports:

| Export | Purpose | Replaces |
|---|---|---|
| `extractOrchestratorRef(params)` | Extract raw orchestrator reference from any of the 4 known param paths | Inline `params.orchestrator_task_id_ref \|\| ...` chains |
| `getSubTaskOrchestratorId(params, taskId)` | Full sub-task detection: extraction + UUID validation + self-reference guard | ~15 lines of inline logic in `calculate-task-cost` and `update-task-status` |
| `buildSubTaskFilter(orchestratorTaskId)` | Build the PostgREST `.or()` filter for finding sub-tasks | 5 duplicated 200+ char filter strings |
| `triggerCostCalculation(url, key, taskId, tag)` | Fire-and-forget HTTP call to `calculate-task-cost` | Duplicate implementations in `complete_task/billing.ts` and `update-task-status` |

### Files modified

1. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`** -- New shared module (128 lines)

2. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/index.ts`** -- Now imports `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` and `buildSubTaskFilter` from `_shared/billing.ts`. Replaced:
   - ~15 lines of inline sub-task detection with a single `getSubTaskOrchestratorId()` call
   - Long inline `.or()` filter with `buildSubTaskFilter(task_id)`

3. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/update-task-status/index.ts`** -- Now imports `extractOrchestratorRef`, `getSubTaskOrchestratorId`, `buildSubTaskFilter`, and `triggerCostCalculation` from `_shared/billing.ts`. Replaced:
   - `triggerOrchestratorBilling` function (32 lines) with shared `triggerCostCalculation`
   - Inline sub-task detection in `handleOrchestratorCancellationBilling` with `getSubTaskOrchestratorId()`
   - Inline orchestrator extraction in `handleCascadingTaskFailure` with `extractOrchestratorRef()`
   - Three inline `.or()` filters with `buildSubTaskFilter()`

4. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/billing.ts`** -- Now imports from `_shared/billing.ts` and re-exports `triggerCostCalculation`. Removed the duplicated `triggerCostCalculation` implementation. Updated `triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask` to use `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` instead of `extractOrchestratorTaskId` (which only checked for presence, not UUID validity or self-reference).

5. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/params.ts`** -- `extractOrchestratorTaskId` now delegates to `extractOrchestratorRef` from `_shared/billing.ts` as the single source of truth for which param paths to check. The logging behavior is preserved as a thin wrapper.

### Behavioral correctness

- No billing behavior changes. The same 4 param paths are checked. The same UUID validation and self-reference guard are applied.
- One minor improvement: `complete_task`'s `triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask` now uses `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` (with UUID validation) instead of raw `extractOrchestratorTaskId`. Previously, a non-UUID orchestrator reference (like a human-readable ID) would cause `complete_task` to incorrectly skip the cost trigger call. In practice this didn't cause billing bugs because `calculate-task-cost` itself would still correctly detect this, but the early-skip logic is now consistent.</result>

## Summary

### What was done

Standardized authentication across 6 edge functions to use the shared `authenticateRequest()` helper from `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/auth.ts`, replacing manual JWT extraction patterns.

### Functions modified

1. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/ai-voice-prompt/index.ts`**
   - Replaced manual `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)` with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[AI-VOICE-PROMPT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Removed manual auth header extraction and token slicing

2. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/generate-pat/index.ts`**
   - Replaced manual `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)` with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[GENERATE-PAT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Changed `user.id` references to `auth.userId`

3. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/revoke-pat/index.ts`**
   - Replaced manual `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)` with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[REVOKE-PAT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Changed `user.id` references to `auth.userId`

4. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/delete-project/index.ts`**
   - Replaced anon-key client + `supabaseClient.auth.getUser()` pattern with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[DELETE-PROJECT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Eliminated the redundant anon-key client (the admin client was already being created separately)
   - Changed `user.id` references to `auth.userId`

5. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/huggingface-upload/index.ts`**
   - Replaced anon-key client + `supabaseClient.auth.getUser()` pattern with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[HF-UPLOAD]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Eliminated the redundant anon-key client
   - Changed `user.id` references to `userId` (local variable from `auth.userId`)

6. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/stripe-checkout/index.ts`**
   - Replaced anon-key client + `supabase.auth.getUser()` pattern with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[STRIPE-CHECKOUT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Eliminated the redundant anon-key client and the conditional service-key check for rate limiting (now always has `supabaseAdmin`)
   - Added an explicit DB lookup for user email (previously obtained from the JWT user object) to preserve the Stripe checkout pre-fill behavior
   - Changed `user.id` and `user.email` references to `auth.userId` and `userData?.email`

7. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/setup-auto-topup/index.ts`**
   - Replaced anon-key client + `supabase.auth.getUser()` pattern with `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[SETUP-AUTO-TOPUP]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`
   - Eliminated the redundant anon-key client; changed `supabase.from(...)` to `supabaseAdmin.from(...)`
   - Changed `user.id` references to `auth.userId`

### Functions intentionally skipped

- **`ai-prompt`** -- Agent P is modifying it in this wave
- **`broadcast-realtime`, `discord-daily-stats`, `generate-thumbnail`, `calculate-task-cost`, `trigger-auto-topup`, `process-auto-topup`, `complete-auto-topup-setup`** -- These are internal/service-role-only functions that use direct service key comparison (not JWT user auth). They should keep their simpler pattern.
- **`stripe-webhook`** -- Uses Stripe signature verification, not JWT
- **`trim-video`** -- Internal service with no explicit auth check (called with service role key by the system)

### Key behavior change

All 6 functions now accept three auth methods (service role key, PAT, or JWT) instead of only JWT. This is a strictly additive change -- the `authenticateRequest` helper with `allowJwtUserAuth: true` accepts all the same JWTs that `auth.getUser()` accepted, plus also accepts service role keys and Personal Access Tokens. This makes these functions accessible via the API (PAT) in addition to the frontend (JWT).

### Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly (the edge functions are Deno-based and excluded from the project's tsconfig, but their import paths match all other existing functions that use `authenticateRequest`)
- No remaining `auth.getUser` calls outside of `ai-prompt` (the one function being skipped)
- All 6 import paths are consistent with the 15 existing functions that already use `authenticateRequest`</result>

<summary>Agent "Wave 4T: App.tsx auth gate" completed</summary>
<result>TypeScript compiles cleanly with zero errors.

Here is a summary of all changes made:

---

## Changes

### 1. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/AuthContext.tsx` (modified)

Added an `isLoading` state to the AuthContext so that consumers can distinguish between "auth is still resolving" and "auth resolved, user is not logged in."

- Added `isLoading: boolean` to `AuthContextType` interface
- Added `const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true)` -- starts as `true`
- Added `setIsLoading(false)` inside the `getSession().then()` callback -- flips to `false` once the initial auth check completes
- Included `isLoading` in the memoized context value

### 2. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/AuthGate.tsx` (new, 15 lines)

A minimal gate component that:
- Calls `useAuth()` to read `isLoading`
- Returns `null` while auth is loading (renders nothing)
- Renders `children` once auth state is known (whether logged in or logged out)
- This is explicitly **not** an authentication wall -- it only delays rendering until the auth check completes

### 3. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/app/App.tsx` (modified)

Inserted `AuthGate` immediately after `AuthProvider`, wrapping all data-dependent providers:

```
QueryClientProvider        -- outside gate (no auth dependency)
  TaskTypeConfigInitializer -- outside gate (config cache)
  AuthProvider             -- provides auth state
    AuthGate               -- NEW: waits for auth to resolve
      UserSettingsProvider  -- needs userId
      ProjectProvider       -- needs userId
      RealtimeProvider      -- needs project/auth
      ShotsProvider         -- needs project
      ...rest               -- all data-dependent
```

The provider ordering is unchanged. The only structural addition is the `AuthGate` wrapper between `AuthProvider` and `UserSettingsProvider`.

### Why this matters

Before this change, all 11 data-dependent providers (UserSettings, Project, Realtime, Shots, etc.) would mount and begin initializing immediately, even while `supabase.auth.getSession()` was still in-flight. This caused wasted renders and potentially wasted network requests (fetching user settings with a null userId, etc.). Now those providers only mount once the auth state is definitively known.</result>

## Over-Engineering Audit Results

### 1. `useMobileTimeoutFallback` hook
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useMobileTimeoutFallback.ts`

**Consumers:** 2 -- `UserSettingsContext.tsx` and `useProjectDefaults.ts`

**Verdict: GOOD**

This is 50 lines including the interface, JSDoc, and the ref-stability pattern. It replaces inline `setTimeout`/`clearTimeout` + `isMobileUA()` boilerplate that would otherwise be duplicated in two contexts. The hook does one thing, the API is minimal (isLoading + onTimeout + optional timeouts + enabled), and the `onTimeoutRef` pattern correctly avoids stale closure bugs. Two consumers is enough to justify extraction -- the alternative is two copies of the same timer/cleanup/ref logic. No issues.

### 2. `createPaneLockSetter` factory in PanesContext
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/PanesContext.tsx`

**Consumers:** Internal only -- creates 3 setters (gens, shots, tasks) at lines 143-145.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The factory (lines 115-141) replaces three nearly-identical functions that each needed: (a) check for no-op, (b) exclusive mobile locking, (c) conditional persistence, (d) tasks pane sync. That is real logic with real branching. Having three copies would mean three places to update if the locking rules change. The factory is parameterized by a single key and returns a setter -- straightforward. The `useMemo` wrapping at lines 143-145 is correct (stable references per key). Clean extraction.

### 3. PaymentSuccessPage state machine
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/pages/PaymentSuccessPage.tsx`

**Verdict: GOOD, borderline unnecessary formalism but net positive**

The "state machine" is really just a `useState<PaymentState>` with 4 states and 2 timers. There is no reducer, no transition table, no external library. The type comment at lines 9-17 documents the transitions, and the actual code uses `setState(prev => prev === 'polling' ? 'verified' : prev)` guards to prevent invalid transitions. This is the right amount of structure for what it does: loading spinner for 1.5s minimum, then success, with a 30s hard timeout. The previous code (if it existed) would have needed the same timers and guards, just with less clarity about what states are possible. 203 lines for a full page with three visual states (error, loading, success) is fine.

### 4. Shared billing module
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`

**Consumers:** 3 edge functions -- `complete_task`, `update-task-status`, `calculate-task-cost`. Plus a local `complete_task/billing.ts` wrapper.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The shared module extracts exactly the right things:
- `extractOrchestratorRef` (4 param paths to check -- real complexity that was duplicated)
- `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` (UUID validation + self-reference guard)
- `buildSubTaskFilter` (4-path PostgREST filter string)
- `triggerCostCalculation` (fire-and-forget HTTP with logging)

These are pure functions with no shared state. The `complete_task/billing.ts` wrapper adds one function (`triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask`) that combines a DB query with the shared logic -- appropriate for a function-specific helper. No coupling problems.

### 5. AuthGate component
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/AuthGate.tsx`

**Consumer:** 1 -- `App.tsx` line 246.

**Verdict: GOOD**

6 lines. Reads `isLoading` from `useAuth()`, returns null while loading, renders children after. This is exactly the right abstraction: it names the intent ("gate data providers until auth is resolved") and keeps App.tsx from needing inline conditionals. Minimal as it gets.

### 6. ProjectContext decomposition
**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx` (thin provider, 128 lines)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectSelection.ts` (150 lines)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectCRUD.ts` (327 lines)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectDefaults.ts` (65 lines)

**Verdict: OVER-ENGINEERED -- mild**

The decomposition itself is reasonable in principle. The original context was presumably 500+ lines doing selection, CRUD, and side-effects. Splitting into three hooks is defensible. However, there are concrete problems:

**Problem 1: Circular dependency between hooks.** `useProjectSelection` imports `determineProjectIdToSelect` from `useProjectCRUD` (line 6). Two hooks that extract from the same context should not depend on each other. `determineProjectIdToSelect` is a pure helper function; it should live in a shared utility or in the context file itself.

**Problem 2: Callback ceremony.** The three hooks communicate through a 7-prop options interface (`useProjectDefaults`) and callback props (`onProjectsLoaded`, `onProjectCreated`, `onProjectDeleted`). The thin provider at lines 38-63 is doing what a single hook body could do -- wiring three sub-hooks together via callbacks. This is the "thin shell" anti-pattern where the decomposition creates more wiring code than it saves in readability.

**Problem 3: `useProjectDefaults` is a bag of side-effects.** It triggers fetches, applies cross-device sync, prefetches tool settings, and runs a mobile timeout fallback. These are 4 unrelated concerns grouped by "they are effects related to projects." The hook name "defaults" does not describe what it does.

**Suggested simplification:** Move `determineProjectIdToSelect` to a shared util. Consider merging `useProjectDefaults` back into the provider (it is only 65 lines and its only consumer is the provider). That leaves a clean two-hook split: selection logic and CRUD logic, composed in the provider.

### 7. `useDeviceInfo()` composite hook
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/use-mobile.tsx`

**Consumers of `useDeviceInfo`:** 3 -- `useLayoutMode.ts`, `ShotEditor/index.tsx`, `SharedGenerationView.tsx`

**Verdict: OVER-ENGINEERED**

**Problem 1: Redundant event listeners.** `useDeviceInfo` calls `useIsMobile()` and `useIsTablet()` internally, which each set up their own resize/matchMedia listeners. Then `useDeviceInfo` adds *another* resize listener (line 219) for `isTabletOrLarger` and `isPortraitMode`. That is 3 resize event listeners per component that uses `useDeviceInfo`. The compute functions (`computeIsMobile`, `computeIsTablet`, etc.) also repeat the same iPad/tablet UA detection logic 3+ times with slightly different formulations.

**Problem 2: `isTouchDevice` is computed wrong in the composite.** At line 198-201, `isTouchDevice` is a `useMemo` that depends on `isMobile` -- but `computeIsTouchDevice()` reads from `window` directly. So it recomputes when `isMobile` changes, but not when the actual touch capability changes (which it never will mid-session anyway). Meanwhile, `useIsTouchDevice()` (the standalone hook) adds a resize listener. These are two different behaviors for the same concept.

**Problem 3: Exports nobody uses.** `useIsPhone()` has zero external consumers. `useIsTouchDevice()` has 1 consumer. These were added speculatively.

**Problem 4: Consumers don't need the composite.** Looking at actual usage:
- `useLayoutMode.ts` uses `isTabletOrLarger`, `isPortraitMode`, `isTouchDevice` -- could use individual hooks or inline checks
- `ShotEditor` uses `isPhone`, `mobileColumns` -- already calls `useIsMobile()` separately on the same line
- `SharedGenerationView` uses only `mobileColumns` -- already calls `useIsMobile()` separately

The `mobileColumns` computation is the only thing the composite actually provides that the individual hooks don't. That could be a 5-line `useMobileColumns()` hook instead of a 60-line god hook.

**Suggested simplification:** Delete `useDeviceInfo`, `useIsPhone`, and the standalone `useIsTouchDevice` (1 consumer can inline it). Extract `useMobileColumns(isMobile, isTablet, orientation)` if needed. Keep the individual hooks (`useIsMobile`, `useIsTablet`) that have real consumers. Deduplicate the iPad detection logic in the compute functions.

---

|------|---------|---------------|
| `useMobileTimeoutFallback` | GOOD | None |
| `createPaneLockSetter` factory | GOOD | None |
| PaymentSuccessPage state machine | GOOD | None |
| Shared billing module | GOOD | None |
| AuthGate | GOOD | None |

| `useDeviceInfo` composite | OVER-ENGINEERED | Delete composite hook; extract only `useMobileColumns` if needed; remove dead exports (`useIsPhone`); deduplicate iPad detection |

5 out of 7 items are clean. The two over-engineered items are both cases of premature abstraction -- creating combinatorial hooks/decompositions before the consumer count justifies them. The device detection file is the more urgent fix due to the redundant event listener problem.</result>

## Behavioral Regression Audit Report

### 1. Task Status Transition Guards (`update-task-status/index.ts`)

**Verdict: RISK -- Minor behavioral tightening, intentional but worth noting**

The refactoring replaced two ad-hoc guards with a comprehensive `validTransitions` lookup table. Here is what changed:

**Old code** had exactly two guards:
- `Complete -> Failed` or `Complete -> Cancelled`: blocked (400)
- `Cancelled -> *anything*`: blocked (400)

Everything else was allowed, including:
- `Failed -> Queued` (allowed -- no guard)
- `Failed -> In Progress` (allowed -- no guard)
- `Failed -> Cancelled` (allowed -- no guard)
- `Complete -> In Progress` (allowed -- no guard)
- `Complete -> Queued` (allowed -- no guard)

**New code** locks down all terminal states:
```
Queued      -> [In Progress, Failed, Cancelled]
In Progress -> [Complete, Failed, Cancelled, Queued]
Complete    -> []      (no transitions)
Failed      -> []      (no transitions)
Cancelled   -> []      (no transitions)
```

**Newly blocked transitions**:
- `Failed -> *anything*`: Previously allowed (a worker could theoretically update a Failed task to Queued or In Progress). Now blocked. The comment says "retries create NEW tasks" which is the intended pattern, confirmed by the `func_reset_orphaned_tasks` migration that resets stuck In Progress tasks back to Queued (not Failed ones). **This is intentional and correct.**
- `Complete -> Queued` / `Complete -> In Progress`: Previously allowed (nonsensical but possible). Now blocked. **Correct.**
- `Queued -> Complete`: Previously allowed (skip straight to complete). Now blocked. This should never happen in practice since tasks go through In Progress first, but it is technically a behavioral change. **Very low risk -- no legitimate use case.**

**Key positive**: `In Progress -> Queued` is correctly allowed for requeue scenarios. The `clear_worker` flag still works as expected.

**Status code change**: Invalid transitions now return 409 (Conflict) instead of 400 (Bad Request). This is a better HTTP semantic but could theoretically affect client error handling if clients match on status code 400 specifically. **Low risk**.

**Overall**: This is a **tightening** of the state machine that removes previously-open loopholes. All legitimate transitions are preserved. The only potential issue is if some external system was relying on `Failed -> *` being allowed, but the codebase design says retries create new tasks, so this is correct.

---

### 2. Rate Limiting Change (`ai-prompt/index.ts`)

**Verdict: SAFE**

The code flow at `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/ai-prompt/index.ts` (lines 36-71):

1. **Auth header check** (line 47): Missing `Bearer` token returns 401 immediately
2. **JWT verification** (line 55): `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)` -- if auth fails, returns 401 immediately (line 58)
3. **Rate limit** (line 62): Only reached after successful auth, uses `user.id`

The order is correct: authentication happens before rate limiting. Unauthenticated requests are rejected with 401 before any rate limit check occurs. This means:
- Unauthenticated requests do **not** consume rate limit budget
- Rate limiting is now per-user instead of per-IP, which is a deliberate improvement (prevents shared-IP rate limit collisions; also means a single user can't evade limits by rotating IPs)

Note: `ai-prompt` does **not** use the shared `authenticateRequest()` module. It uses its own inline JWT verification via `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser()`. This is inconsistent with the rest of the codebase but is not a regression since this function was not part of the auth standardization wave (it uses Supabase's native JWT verification which is actually more secure than the manual JWT decode in `authenticateRequest`).

---

### 3. Auth Standardization

**Verdict: SAFE**

I examined multiple functions that were migrated to `authenticateRequest()`:

**`claim-next-task/index.ts`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`):
- Auth at line 81, before any DB work -- correct placement
- Error response uses `auth.statusCode || 403` -- matches expected behavior
- `auth.isServiceRole` and `auth.userId` properly used downstream

**`create-task/index.ts`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/create-task/index.ts`):
- Auth at line 120 with `{ allowJwtUserAuth: true }` -- enables frontend JWT auth
- Properly separates service-role vs user-token paths
- Rate limiting correctly skipped for service role (line 140)

**`get-task-status/index.ts`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/get-task-status/index.ts`):
- Auth at line 55, after body parsing but before DB queries -- correct
- Error handling delegates to `auth.statusCode` -- correct

**`generate-upload-url/index.ts`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/generate-upload-url/index.ts`):
- Auth at line 84, uses both `verifyTaskOwnership` and `getTaskUserId` helpers from shared auth module -- clean delegation

**`authenticateRequest()` itself** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/auth.ts`):
- Checks in order: (1) service-role key exact match, (2) JWT decode (optional), (3) PAT lookup
- Returns appropriate status codes: 401 for missing header, 500 for config issues, 403 for invalid token
- Importantly: JWT user auth is **opt-in** via `allowJwtUserAuth` flag, so functions that only expect PAT/service-key won't accidentally accept JWTs
- **Security note**: The JWT decode at line 90-106 only decodes the payload without cryptographic verification (`atob` of the payload section). This is acceptable because: (a) service_role/supabase_admin claims are explicitly rejected, and (b) Supabase's gateway already validates JWTs before they reach edge functions. But it's worth noting that this is a weaker verification than `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)`.

**One subtlety**: The old inline auth patterns typically returned 401 for both missing and invalid tokens. The shared module returns 401 for missing headers but 403 for invalid tokens (failed PAT lookup). This is actually more semantically correct (401 = "who are you?" vs 403 = "I know who you are and you don't have access"), but could theoretically affect clients that rely on specific status codes. **Very low risk**.

---

### 4. ProjectContext Decomposition

**Verdict: SAFE**

The `ProjectContext` (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx`) was decomposed into three hooks:

- **`useProjectSelection`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectSelection.ts`): Manages `selectedProjectId`, localStorage fast-resume, cross-device sync
- **`useProjectCRUD`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectCRUD.ts`): Manages projects list, fetch, create, update, delete
- **`useProjectDefaults`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectDefaults.ts`): Side effects -- fetch trigger, cross-device sync, prefetch, mobile fallback

**Context shape verification** -- The `ProjectContextType` interface at line 15-29 exposes:
- `projects`, `selectedProjectId`, `setSelectedProjectId`, `isLoadingProjects`, `fetchProjects`
- `addNewProject`, `isCreatingProject`, `updateProject`, `isUpdatingProject`
- `deleteProject`, `isDeletingProject`, `userId`

All 46 consumers use `useProject()` which returns this exact shape. The shape has not changed.

**Project selection persistence**: `handleSetSelectedProjectId` (line 86 of `useProjectSelection.ts`) still writes to both `localStorage` and server preferences (`updateUserSettings`). Fast resume from localStorage is preserved. Cross-device sync is preserved.

**One subtle concern in the decomposition**: In `useProjectCRUD.ts` line 300-303, `deleteProject` calls `onProjectDeleted(updated)` inside a `setProjects` updater function. This means the parent's `handleProjectDeleted` callback runs synchronously during a React state update, which can work but is a React anti-pattern (calling external callbacks during state updates). However, this pattern was likely present in the original monolithic component too, and React batches updates, so in practice this works fine. **Not a regression**.

---

### 5. Billing Extraction

**Verdict: SAFE**

The shared billing module (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`) extracts three key functions:

**`extractOrchestratorRef(params)`** (line 31-41): Checks all 4 param paths:
1. `params.orchestrator_task_id_ref` -- travel_segment
2. `params.orchestrator_details?.orchestrator_task_id` -- join_clips_segment
3. `params.originalParams?.orchestrator_details?.orchestrator_task_id` -- legacy
4. `params.orchestrator_task_id` -- some legacy tasks

**Verified against old inline code**: The old `handleOrchestratorCancellationBilling` (from the pre-refactor version) had the same 4 paths in the same order:
```typescript
const orchestratorRef = params.orchestrator_task_id_ref || 
                        params.orchestrator_details?.orchestrator_task_id ||
                        params.originalParams?.orchestrator_details?.orchestrator_task_id ||
                        params.orchestrator_task_id;
```
This is an exact match with `extractOrchestratorRef`.

**`getSubTaskOrchestratorId(params, taskId)`** (line 56-62): Wraps `extractOrchestratorRef` with two additional guards:
1. UUID validation (`UUID_REGEX.test(ref)`) -- prevents human-readable IDs from being treated as orchestrator refs
2. Self-reference guard (`ref === taskId`) -- prevents orchestrators from being identified as their own sub-tasks

Both guards were present inline in the old code (UUID regex check and `orchestratorRef !== cancelledTaskId`). **Exact behavioral match**.

**`buildSubTaskFilter(orchestratorTaskId)`** (line 75-82): Generates the PostgREST `.or()` filter string for all 4 param paths. The old code had this same filter inlined in multiple places (the `.or(...)` call in `handleOrchestratorCancellationBilling` and `handleCascadingTaskFailure`). **Exact match**.

**Consumers verified**:
- `calculate-task-cost/index.ts` (line 209): Uses `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` to skip billing for sub-tasks, and `buildSubTaskFilter` to find sub-tasks of orchestrators
- `update-task-status/index.ts` (line 63): Uses `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` in cancellation billing; uses `extractOrchestratorRef` in cascade handler; uses `buildSubTaskFilter` for queries
- `complete_task/billing.ts` (line 37): Re-exports and wraps `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` + `triggerCostCalculation`

The extraction is a clean refactor with no behavioral change.

---

## Summary

| Area | Verdict | Notes |
|------|---------|-------|
| Task status transitions | **SAFE (minor tightening)** | Terminal states now fully locked. `Failed -> *` newly blocked but matches documented design. All legitimate worker/user flows preserved. Status code 400 -> 409 for invalid transitions. |
| Rate limiting (ai-prompt) | **SAFE** | Auth correctly precedes rate limit. Unauthenticated requests rejected before rate limit check. User-based rate limiting is a deliberate improvement. |
| Auth standardization | **SAFE** | Same flow order, similar error responses. Minor 401/403 refinement is more correct. JWT user auth is opt-in. |
| ProjectContext decomposition | **SAFE** | Context shape unchanged. All 46 consumers unaffected. Selection persistence, cross-device sync, and all CRUD operations preserved. |
| Billing extraction | **SAFE** | All 4 param paths preserved in exact same order. UUID validation and self-reference guards preserved. Query filters identical. No behavioral change. |</result>

     ```tsx
       (lockKey: 'gens' | 'shots' | 'tasks') => (isLocked: boolean) => {
         setLocks(prev => {
           if (prev[lockKey] === isLocked) return prev;
           const exclusiveLock = (isMobile || isTablet) && isLocked;
           return exclusiveLock
             ? { shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }
             : { ...prev, [lockKey]: isLocked };
         });
         // Save outside state updater — avoids side effects in updater functions
         if (!isSmallMobile) {
           const exclusiveLock = (isMobile || isTablet) && isLocked;
           savePaneLocks(exclusiveLock
             ? { shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }
             : { [lockKey]: isLocked });
         }
         if (lockKey === 'tasks' && isLocked) {
           setIsTasksPaneOpenState(true);
         }
       },
       [savePaneLocks, isSmallMobile, isMobile, isTablet]
     );
     ```

     ```tsx
     export const ImageEditProvider: React.FC<ImageEditProviderProps> = ({ children, value }) => {
       const canvasValue = useMemo<ImageEditCanvasState>(() => ({...}), [...40 deps]);
       const formValue = useMemo<ImageEditFormState>(() => ({...}), [...20 deps]);
       const statusValue = useMemo<ImageEditStatusState>(() => ({...}), [...10 deps]);
       return (
         <ImageEditCanvasProvider value={canvasValue}>
           <ImageEditFormProvider value={formValue}>
             <ImageEditStatusProvider value={statusValue}>
               {children}
             </ImageEditStatusProvider>
           </ImageEditFormProvider>
         </ImageEditCanvasProvider>
       );
     };
     ```

### Pattern Applied

For each function:
1. Added `import { SystemLogger } from "../_shared/systemLogger.ts";`
2. Created `const logger = new SystemLogger(supabaseAdmin, 'function-name');` after the Supabase admin client initialization
3. Replaced `console.log(...)` with `logger.info(...)`, `console.error(...)` with `logger.error(...)`, `console.warn(...)` with `logger.warn(...)`
4. Converted log messages to use structured context objects (e.g., `{ user_id, error: err.message }`) instead of string interpolation
5. Added `await logger.flush();` before every `return new Response(...)` in error paths and successful completion paths
6. Ensured the logger is created in a scope accessible to both `try` and `catch` blocks (moved supabase client + logger creation before the `try` block where needed)

- **Pre-client-init errors**: Several functions have `console.error("Missing required environment variables")` that runs before the Supabase client can be created. These must stay as `console.error` since there's no logger available yet.
- **trim-video standalone helper functions**: Functions like `waitForPrediction`, `trimWithReplicate`, `downloadVideo`, `uploadToStorage`, `updateVariantRecord` use `console.log` with consistent `[TRIM-VIDEO]` tags. Passing a logger parameter to all of them would change their function signatures, which goes beyond mechanical standardization.
- **broadcast-realtime outer catch**: The catch block falls back to `console.error` since the logger is created inside the try block for this function. Only the catch is affected; the happy and error paths within the try use the logger.
- **ai-voice-prompt GROQ_API_KEY check**: Module-init-time console.error before any request is handled.

### CORS Status

No shared CORS helper exists. All functions use inline CORS headers in one of two patterns:
- A `corsHeaders` constant with spread into response headers
- A `jsonResponse()` helper that includes CORS headers

As instructed, I did not create a shared CORS helper since one doesn't already exist.

### Verification

`npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly with zero errors. No business logic was changed -- only logging calls were migrated to use the shared `SystemLogger` pattern.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review: Subtle regression hunting" completed</summary>
<result>I now have a thorough understanding of all six areas. Here is the detailed review.

---

## Detailed Review: 6 Areas of Concern

---

### 1. complete_task FK Join

**Files:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts` (lines 43-69), `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/migrations/20260213000000_add_tasks_task_type_fkey.sql`

**Old code** (pre-Wave 1):
```ts
// Two separate queries
const { data: task } = await supabase.from("tasks").select(`id, task_type, project_id, params`).eq("id", taskId).single();
const { data: typeData } = await supabase.from("task_types").select("tool_type, category, content_type, variant_type").eq("name", task.task_type).single();
```

**New code:**
```ts
const { data: task } = await supabase
  .from("tasks")
  .select(`id, task_type, project_id, params, task_types!tasks_task_type_fkey(tool_type, category, content_type, variant_type)`)
  .eq("id", taskId)
  .single();
const taskTypeInfo = task.task_types || {};
```

**Sub-question A: What if the FK migration hasn't been run?**

If the migration `20260213000000_add_tasks_task_type_fkey.sql` has not been applied, PostgREST will not recognize the `!tasks_task_type_fkey` hint and will return an HTTP error (typically `400 Bad Request` with a message like "Could not find a relationship between 'tasks' and 'task_types' in the schema cache"). This would cause `error` to be truthy, so `fetchTaskContext` returns `null`, and the function returns a 404 "Task not found." The task would fail to complete, and the 500 error message would be misleading (it says "Task not found" rather than "schema relationship missing"). However, this is a deploy ordering issue -- the migration must be deployed before the edge function.

**Sub-question B: What if task_type is null or doesn't match any task_types row?**

The `tasks.task_type` column is `text NOT NULL` (confirmed in base schema migration), so it will never be null. If the value doesn't match any `task_types.name`, PostgREST with a `!fkey` hint on a to-one relationship will return `task_types` as `null` (not an error) because the FK relationship will simply find no matching row. The code handles this gracefully with `const taskTypeInfo = task.task_types || {};` followed by fallbacks like `taskTypeInfo.tool_type || 'unknown'`.

**Sub-question C: Does PostgREST return the joined data in the same shape the code expects?**

PostgREST returns a to-one FK join as an embedded object. Since `tasks.task_type` references `task_types.name`, the join is many-to-one. PostgREST will return `task.task_types` as an object (not an array) -- e.g., `{ tool_type: "image-gen", category: "generation", ... }`. The code accesses it as `task.task_types.tool_type` which is correct for this shape. If the FK constraint has `ON DELETE SET NULL` semantics (it does not -- this is a simple FK with no cascade), then the join could return `null` if the referenced row is deleted, but the `|| {}` fallback handles this.

**Verdict: POTENTIAL ISSUE -- deploy ordering dependency.** The edge function MUST NOT be deployed before the migration is run. If the function is deployed first (or the function is restarted before PostgREST's schema cache refreshes), all task completions will fail with a confusing "Task not found" error. The old two-query code was resilient to the FK not existing. This is a deployment coupling risk, not a code bug, but it deserves a comment or a deploy-order note. In steady state, the code is correct and safe.

---

### 2. PanesContext Factory -- Side Effect Inside State Updater

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/PanesContext.tsx` (lines 115-141)

```ts
const createPaneLockSetter = useCallback(
  (lockKey: 'gens' | 'shots' | 'tasks') => (isLocked: boolean) => {
    setLocks(prev => {
      if (prev[lockKey] === isLocked) return prev;
      const exclusiveLock = (isMobile || isTablet) && isLocked;
      const newLocks = exclusiveLock
        ? { shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }
        : { ...prev, [lockKey]: isLocked };

      // Side effect inside state updater:
      if (!isSmallMobile) {
        savePaneLocks(exclusiveLock ? newLocks : { [lockKey]: isLocked });
      }

      return newLocks;
    });
    // ...
  },
  [savePaneLocks, isSmallMobile, isMobile, isTablet]
);
```

**Analysis:** `savePaneLocks` is the `update` function from `useUserUIState`. Looking at its implementation (line 235 of `useUserUIState.ts`), it does: (1) immediately calls `setValue(normalizedPatch)` (another React state setter), and (2) schedules a debounced `setTimeout` for the DB write. Calling it inside a `setLocks` state updater means:

1. **Calling `setValue` from inside another state updater.** In React 18 with automatic batching, calling one state setter inside another setter's updater function is technically supported -- React batches these. However, it is an anti-pattern because React's docs explicitly warn that state updater functions should be pure (no side effects). React may call them multiple times in development Strict Mode, which would trigger multiple `savePaneLocks` calls.

2. **StrictMode double invocation.** In development with StrictMode, React intentionally double-invokes state updaters to detect impure functions. This means `savePaneLocks` would be called twice per lock toggle in dev mode. The second call would re-schedule the debounce timer (overwriting the first), so the DB write itself would only happen once. But the local `setValue` inside `update` would be called twice.

3. **Stale closure on `savePaneLocks`.** The `savePaneLocks` captured in the `useCallback` closure is from the `useUserUIState` hook's `update` function. The `update` function itself closes over `value` (the current state of the pane locks). When `savePaneLocks` merges `{ ...value, ...patch }`, it uses the `value` from the last render. But since `setLocks(prev => ...)` computes `newLocks` from the `prev` state (correct), and then passes `newLocks` (or a subset) to `savePaneLocks`, the merge inside `savePaneLocks` does `{ ...value, ...patch }` where `patch` is the correct new value. Since the patch contains the authoritative key, the stale `value` base doesn't matter for the keys being updated.

**Verdict: POTENTIAL ISSUE -- anti-pattern, but not a production bug.** The side effect inside the state updater violates React's purity contract for updater functions. In practice, the double-invocation in StrictMode will cause a harmless extra `setValue` call (which React batches) and an extra `setTimeout` that overwrites the first (so the DB write is fine). In production mode, where updaters are only called once, this works correctly. The code should ideally move the `savePaneLocks` call outside the `setLocks` updater (compute `newLocks`, then call `setLocks(() => newLocks)` and `savePaneLocks(newLocks)` separately), but it will not cause bugs in production.

---

### 3. PaymentSuccessPage -- Missing Auto-Redirect

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/pages/PaymentSuccessPage.tsx`

**Old code analysis:**
```ts
// Old code had verificationStatus in useEffect deps, causing re-runs:
useEffect(() => {
  // ...
  setTimeout(() => { setVerificationStatus('success'); }, 1500);
  // ...
}, [sessionId, queryClient, searchParams, verificationStatus]);
// No auto-redirect to /tools — user always clicked "Return to Tool" button
```

**New code analysis:**
The new code uses a proper state machine (`loading -> polling -> verified / timed_out`) with clear transitions. There is no auto-redirect in either version -- both old and new require the user to click "Return to Tool" or "View Transactions."

**Key difference:** The old code had `verificationStatus` in its useEffect dependency array, which is a bug. When `verificationStatus` changed from `'loading'` to `'success'`, the effect would re-run, creating duplicate timers and intervals. The new code correctly runs the effect once (on mount) and uses separate effects for state-driven cleanup.

**Old code also had no auto-redirect.** I verified there is no `navigate()` call inside any `setTimeout` or automatic transition. Both versions show the success screen and wait for user interaction.

**Verdict: NO ISSUE.** The refactor is a strict improvement. No auto-redirect existed before, so none was removed. The old code's `verificationStatus` dependency bug (causing re-run loops and duplicate timers) is fixed. The new state machine is cleaner and correctly cleans up all timers.

---

### 4. Auth Standardization in Edge Functions

**Files:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/stripe-checkout/index.ts`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/setup-auto-topup/index.ts`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/auth.ts`

**stripe-checkout (line 89):**
```ts
const supabaseAdmin = createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey);
const auth = await authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[STRIPE-CHECKOUT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true });
```

The `authenticateRequest` function with `allowJwtUserAuth: true` decodes the JWT payload to extract the user ID (`payload.sub`) but does NOT create a user-scoped Supabase client. The `supabaseAdmin` client (service role) is used for all subsequent queries.

**RLS implications for stripe-checkout:** The only DB query after auth is `supabaseAdmin.from('users').select('email').eq('id', auth.userId).single()` (line 128). This reads a user's email for Stripe checkout pre-fill. With the service role client, this bypasses RLS but is filtered by `.eq('id', auth.userId)` -- the user can only read their own email because the authenticated `userId` is derived from their JWT. There is no privilege escalation because the query is scoped to the authenticated user's ID.

**setup-auto-topup (line 126-129):**
```ts
const { error: updateError } = await supabaseAdmin.from('users').update(updateData).eq('id', auth.userId);
```

This updates the authenticated user's own record using `auth.userId` from JWT verification. The `.eq('id', auth.userId)` filter ensures only the authenticated user's row is modified. The only risk would be if `auth.userId` could be spoofed, but `authenticateRequest` derives it from the JWT `sub` claim, which is signed by Supabase. An attacker would need a valid JWT for a different user.

**One concern with JWT auth:** The `authenticateRequest` function decodes the JWT payload without cryptographic verification (line 91-107 of `auth.ts`). It uses `atob(padded)` to decode the payload and trusts `payload.sub`. This is only safe because Supabase edge functions receive the JWT via the API gateway, which has already validated the JWT signature. If this function were ever called directly (bypassing the API gateway), the JWT would not be verified. However, this is the standard Supabase edge function pattern and is documented as intentional.

**Verdict: NO ISSUE.** Both functions correctly scope all DB operations to the authenticated user's ID. The service role client bypasses RLS but the `.eq('id', auth.userId)` filter provides equivalent protection. The JWT decode-without-verify pattern is safe within Supabase edge functions (the gateway validates signatures).

---

### 5. Window Globals DEV Gating

**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx` (line 67-68)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/TasksPane/TasksPane.tsx` (line 42-43)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/integrations/supabase/auth/AuthStateManager.ts` (line 82-85)

**`window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__`:** Assigned only in DEV mode (line 67: `if (import.meta.env.DEV && typeof window !== 'undefined')`). However, it is **read in production code paths**:

- `src/shared/hooks/useProjectGenerations.ts:400` -- `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__?.selectedProjectId` as a fallback when `projectId` is not passed.
- `src/shared/hooks/useTasks.ts:117` -- same pattern.
- `src/tools/image-generation/pages/ImageGenerationToolPage.tsx:43` -- same pattern.

In production, since `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` is never assigned (DEV gate), these reads will return `undefined`, and the optional chaining (`?.selectedProjectId`) will safely yield `undefined`. The code then falls through to other fallbacks (localStorage, `null`). **This means in production, this fallback path is dead code** -- it will never provide a project ID. Whether this is a problem depends on whether the `projectId` parameter is always provided in production. If `projectId` is sometimes `undefined` in production AND the localStorage fallback also fails, the query would run with `projectId: null`, which could cause incorrect behavior.

**`window.__AUTH_MANAGER__`:** NOT DEV-gated. Looking at `AuthStateManager.ts` line 82-85: `window.__AUTH_MANAGER__ = new AuthStateManager(supabase)` runs unconditionally (only checks `typeof window !== 'undefined'`). The reading code in `AuthContext.tsx`, `useAuthGuard.ts`, `useHomeAuth.ts`, and `useGlobalHeaderAuth.ts` all access it unconditionally in production. This is correct -- it is categorized as "[structural]" in `browser-extensions.d.ts`.

**`window.__REACT_QUERY_CLIENT__`:** DEV-gated in `TasksPane.tsx` (line 42: `if (import.meta.env.DEV && ...)`). I found no production code that reads this global. It is categorized as "[debug]" in `browser-extensions.d.ts`.

**Verdict: BUG -- `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` is DEV-gated on writes but read in production code.** In production, the three reader sites (`useProjectGenerations`, `useTasks`, `ImageGenerationToolPage`) will always get `undefined` from this global. If `projectId` is sometimes `null/undefined` in production, the intended fallback to the global is broken. The `ImageGenerationToolPage` has a further fallback to `localStorage`, but `useProjectGenerations` and `useTasks` do not -- they would proceed with `effectiveProjectId: null/undefined`, which could cause queries to run without a project filter or be disabled.

This should be investigated: either (a) the DEV gate on the write should be removed (since this is a "[structural]" global per the type file), or (b) the production read sites should be updated to not rely on this global (since it was classified as structural but is now DEV-only).

---

### 6. useMobileTimeoutFallback -- Cleanup and Race Conditions

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useMobileTimeoutFallback.ts`

```ts
export function useMobileTimeoutFallback({
  isLoading, onTimeout, mobileTimeoutMs = 15000, desktopTimeoutMs = 10000, enabled = true,
}: UseMobileTimeoutFallbackOptions): void {
  const onTimeoutRef = useRef(onTimeout);
  onTimeoutRef.current = onTimeout;

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!enabled || !isLoading) return;

    const isMobile = isMobileUA();
    const timeoutMs = isMobile ? mobileTimeoutMs : desktopTimeoutMs;

    const timer = setTimeout(() => {
      onTimeoutRef.current();
    }, timeoutMs);

    return () => {
      clearTimeout(timer);
    };
  }, [isLoading, enabled, mobileTimeoutMs, desktopTimeoutMs]);
}
```

**Cleanup on unmount:** The effect returns `() => { clearTimeout(timer); }`. React calls this cleanup function both when deps change AND on unmount. So if the component unmounts while `isLoading` is true and the timer is pending, the timer is properly cleared.

**Race condition between timeout firing and component unmounting:** If the timer fires just before React runs the cleanup, `onTimeoutRef.current()` will execute. At this point:
- The component is still mounted (cleanup hasn't run yet).
- `onTimeoutRef.current` points to the latest `onTimeout` callback.
- The callback will run (e.g., `fetchProjects()` in `useProjectDefaults`, or `setIsLoadingSettings(false)` in `UserSettingsContext`).

This is a standard React pattern. The `ref` ensures no stale closure, and `clearTimeout` in cleanup prevents the callback from running if the timeout hasn't fired yet. There is a theoretical micro-race where `setTimeout` fires its macrotask, React hasn't run cleanup yet, and the callback executes on an about-to-unmount component. But in React, state updates on unmounted components are either batched (React 18) or result in a harmless no-op warning (React 17). Both callers set state, which is safe.

**`onTimeout` ref pattern:** The `useRef` pattern for `onTimeout` is correct and avoids the stale-closure problem. The ref is updated on every render (`onTimeoutRef.current = onTimeout`), so the timeout always calls the latest callback.

**Verdict: NO ISSUE.** The hook correctly cleans up timeouts on unmount and on dependency changes. The ref pattern prevents stale closures. The theoretical race between timeout firing and unmount is handled safely by React's batching.

---

## Summary Table

| # | Area | Verdict | Severity |
|---|------|---------|----------|
| 1 | complete_task FK join | **POTENTIAL ISSUE** | Medium -- deploy ordering dependency. If edge function deployed before migration, all task completions fail with misleading error. |
| 2 | PanesContext factory side effect | **POTENTIAL ISSUE** | Low -- anti-pattern (side effect in state updater), causes harmless double-calls in StrictMode dev. No production bug. |
| 3 | PaymentSuccessPage auto-redirect | **NO ISSUE** | N/A -- neither old nor new code had auto-redirect. The refactor fixes a dependency-array bug in the old code. |
| 4 | Auth standardization (stripe-checkout, setup-auto-topup) | **NO ISSUE** | N/A -- service role usage is safe because all queries are scoped to authenticated user ID. |
| 5 | Window globals DEV gating | **BUG** | High -- `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` is DEV-gated on writes but read in production by `useProjectGenerations`, `useTasks`, and `ImageGenerationToolPage`. The fallback path is silently broken in production. |
| 6 | useMobileTimeoutFallback | **NO ISSUE** | N/A -- proper cleanup, no stale closures, standard React timeout pattern. |

**The item requiring immediate attention is #5.** The `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` DEV gate on the write side (`ProjectContext.tsx` line 67) breaks production fallback paths in three consumer files. Either remove the DEV gate (since the type file classifies it as `[structural]`), or audit whether those fallback reads are actually ever reached in production and remove them if they are dead code.</result>

## Decomposition Summary

### New structure after decomposition

  - Owns top-level UI state (submit success, saving defaults, drag state)

**Extracted sub-components:**

   - The collapsible "Advanced Settings" panel
   - Before/after prompt text fields, negative prompt

   - Delegates structure video rendering to `StructureVideoSection`
   - LoRA selector modal
   - Owns its own `showAdvanced`, `isLoraModalOpen`, and `savingField` state

### What was preserved

- **No files outside the directory were touched**
