<result>Clean. Zero errors.

---

Here is a summary of the decomposition.

## What was done

The monolithic `ImageEditContext` (319 lines, 70+ fields in a single React Context) was decomposed into three sub-contexts grouped by concern, with a backward-compatible composed provider.

### New files created

1. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditCanvasContext.tsx`** (~185 lines)
   - Contains: edit mode, brush tool, annotation tool, canvas interaction, reposition, display refs, panel position
   - Hook: `useImageEditCanvasSafe()`
   - Consumers: LightboxLayout, FloatingToolControls, MediaDisplayWithCanvas, InfoPanel

2. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditFormContext.tsx`** (~105 lines)
   - Contains: prompts, strengths, LoRA, model selection, advanced settings, numGenerations, createAsGeneration
   - Hook: `useImageEditFormSafe()`
   - Consumer: EditModePanel only

3. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditStatusContext.tsx`** (~80 lines)
   - Contains: generation loading/success flags (10 booleans)
   - Hook: `useImageEditStatusSafe()`
   - Consumer: EditModePanel only

### Modified files

4. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditContext.tsx`** (319 -> 233 lines)
   - Rewritten as a composed provider that splits a single `ImageEditState` value into three sub-context slices via `useMemo`
   - `ImageEditState` type is now `ImageEditCanvasState & ImageEditFormState & ImageEditStatusState`
   - `ImageEditProvider` nests all three sub-providers
   - `useImageEditSafe()` still works (merges all three sub-contexts) but is marked as the backward-compatible option
   - All sub-context types and hooks are re-exported for discoverability

5. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/FloatingToolControls.tsx`**
   - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs canvas state)

6. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/MediaDisplayWithCanvas.tsx`**
   - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs canvas state)

7. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/layouts/LightboxLayout.tsx`**
   - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs canvas state)

8. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/InfoPanel.tsx`**
   - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs 3 mode fields)

9. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/EditModePanel.tsx`**
   - Changed from single `useImageEditSafe()` to three specific hooks: `useImageEditCanvasSafe()`, `useImageEditFormSafe()`, `useImageEditStatusSafe()`
   - Props-first override pattern (`imageEditState ?? context`) preserved for InlineEditView compatibility

### Re-render reduction

The key performance win: **canvas/overlay components (LightboxLayout, FloatingToolControls, MediaDisplayWithCanvas) no longer re-render when form fields change** (user typing prompts, adjusting strength sliders, toggling LoRA, etc.), and vice versa. The `ImageEditProvider` splits the incoming value into three `useMemo`-guarded slices, so only the relevant sub-context value changes when its fields change.

### Why 3 contexts, not 4

The original plan suggested 4 contexts (EditMode, BrushTool, CanvasInteraction, EditForm). After analyzing all consumers, I found that mode/brush/annotation/canvas/reposition fields are consumed together by LightboxLayout, FloatingToolControls, and MediaDisplayWithCanvas. Splitting these into separate contexts would not reduce re-renders because every canvas-related component needs fields from all of them. The natural boundary is **canvas vs. form vs. status** -- form and status are only consumed by EditModePanel, while canvas is consumed by all the overlay/layout components.</result>

## Summary

### Problem
`MediaGallery` had 49 top-level props in its `MediaGalleryProps` interface, of which 17 were boolean configuration flags controlling visibility, layout modes, and section hiding.

### Solution
Created a `GalleryConfig` interface grouping all 17 boolean flags into a single `config?: Partial<GalleryConfig>` prop. Callers now pass a config object instead of 17 individual boolean props.

### Files Modified

**1. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGallery/types.ts`**
- Added `GalleryConfig` interface with 17 boolean fields organized into three groups:
  - **Visibility flags:** `showDelete`, `showDownload`, `showShare`, `showEdit`, `showStar`, `showAddToShot`
  - **UI mode flags:** `enableSingleClick`, `videosAsThumbnails`, `whiteText`, `reducedSpacing`, `showShotFilter`, `showSearch`
  - **Hide flags:** `hidePagination`, `hideTopFilters`, `hideMediaTypeFilter`, `hideBottomPagination`, `hideShotNotifier`
- Added `DEFAULT_GALLERY_CONFIG` constant with all default values matching previous defaults
- Replaced 17 individual boolean props in `MediaGalleryProps` with `config?: Partial<GalleryConfig>`

**2. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGallery/index.tsx`**
- Added imports for `GalleryConfig` and `DEFAULT_GALLERY_CONFIG`
- Added re-exports for external consumers
- Changed props destructuring to extract `config: configOverrides` instead of 17 individual booleans
- Added `useMemo` to merge `configOverrides` with `DEFAULT_GALLERY_CONFIG`
- Destructured the merged config into local variables for downstream usage (no changes to child component prop passing)

**3-9. Eight caller files updated** (all pass `config={{ ... }}` instead of individual boolean props):
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/GenerationsPane/GenerationsPane.tsx`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/edit-images/pages/EditImagesPage.tsx`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/edit-video/pages/EditVideoPage.tsx` (2 usages)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/character-animate/pages/CharacterAnimatePage.tsx`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/join-clips/pages/JoinClipsPage.tsx`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaSelectionPanel.tsx`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/VideoTravelVideosGallery.tsx`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/image-generation/pages/ImageGenerationToolPage.tsx`

### Prop count reduction
- **Before:** 49 props on `MediaGalleryProps`
- **After:** 32 props on `MediaGalleryProps` (17 booleans consolidated into 1 config object)
- **Reduction:** -35% (17 fewer top-level props)

### No behavioral changes
- All default values preserved exactly as before
- Child components (`MediaGalleryGrid`, `MediaGalleryHeader`, `MediaGalleryLightbox`, `MediaGalleryItem`) receive the same individual props as before -- the config is destructured inside `index.tsx` and passed through
- TypeScript compilation passes cleanly with `npx tsc --noEmit`

### Lightbox extraction assessment
The lightbox-related code in `index.tsx` is approximately 130 lines. Since it's already well-decomposed into `useLightboxNavigation` (hook) and `MediaGalleryLightbox` (component), the remaining code is mostly wiring between hooks. Extracting another layer would add indirection without meaningful simplification, so it was left as-is per the "don't over-abstract" principle.</result>

## Over-Engineering Review: Waves 1-5a Refactoring

### 1. ImageEditContext Split (Wave 5a)

**Rating: MILD**

**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditCanvasContext.tsx` (182L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditFormContext.tsx` (105L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditStatusContext.tsx` (76L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditContext.tsx` (234L)

**Analysis:**

The split is defensible in principle: canvas operations (brush strokes, annotations) update at high frequency and should not re-render the form panel, and vice versa. The consumers have migrated well -- 5 components use `useImageEditCanvasSafe` directly, and only `EditModePanel` uses all three sub-hooks. Nobody uses the backward-compatible `useImageEditSafe()` anymore (excluding the definition and task files). That is a good sign: the migration is complete.

**However, the useMemo slicing in `ImageEditContext.tsx` is likely ineffective.** The composed provider receives a single `value: ImageEditState` object. That object is constructed upstream (presumably in a hook that creates a new object on every render). The `useMemo` at lines 79-150 lists ~40 individual dependency values for the canvas slice alone. Since the `value` prop is a new object each render, and many of those dependency values are callback references that may not be stable, the useMemo will likely recompute on almost every render anyway. The real render savings would only work if the upstream hook is also carefully memoizing every callback. The pattern adds 120 lines of brittle, hard-to-audit dependency arrays.

The `FormContext` and `StatusContext` each have essentially **one consumer** (`EditModePanel`). Having a separate React context for a single consumer adds overhead (context machinery, provider nesting) with no render savings -- the only component subscribing to those contexts re-renders when either one changes, and it subscribes to both. A simpler approach: keep `CanvasContext` split out (it has 4+ consumers with genuinely different concerns), but merge Form and Status back since they share a single consumer.

**Specific concerns:**
- 150-line useMemo dependency array for `canvasValue` (lines 132-150) is a maintenance hazard. One missed dependency silently breaks memoization.
- `EMPTY_CANVAS` default (lines 93-151) is 58 lines of no-op stubs that duplicate the interface shape. This is defensive but verbose.

**Recommendation:** The 3-way split is slightly over-done given actual consumer patterns. A 2-way split (Canvas vs Everything Else) would have been sufficient. The useMemo slicing should be audited for whether it actually prevents re-renders in practice, or is just ceremony.

---

### 2. SegmentSettingsForm Extraction (Wave 5a)

**Rating: OVER**

**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/SegmentSettingsForm.tsx` (376L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/AdvancedSettingsSection.tsx` (396L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/PromptSection.tsx` (116L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/StructureVideoSection.tsx` (429L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/StructureVideoPreview.tsx` (258L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/FieldDefaultControls.tsx` (63L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/EnhancedPromptBadge.tsx` (66L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/VideoPreviewSkeleton.tsx` (40L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/hooks/useStructureVideoUpload.ts` (247L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/types.ts` (146L)

**Total: 2,142 lines** (up from the claimed 1,044L original -- that is a 2x increase).

**Analysis:**

The extraction went too far. The headline numbers are damning: the decomposition more than doubled the total line count. The claimed 1044 -> 1334 was already a red flag, but the actual total is 2142 lines. Where did the extra 800 lines go?

1. **Prop drilling tax:** `AdvancedSettingsSection` has a 16-property interface (lines 28-59) that is mostly pass-through from the parent. The parent (`SegmentSettingsForm`) still destructures all the same props (lines 44-82, 38 props!) and then re-passes subsets to children. This is the classic "extraction that adds lines" anti-pattern -- the component boundary doesn't reduce complexity, it just adds a prop-passing layer.

2. **Duplicated handler patterns:** `handleSaveFieldAsDefault` is duplicated between `SegmentSettingsForm.tsx` (lines 144-156) and `AdvancedSettingsSection.tsx` (lines 186-198) -- identical logic in both places.

3. **`PromptSection` is too thin:** At 116 lines (mostly JSX), it does not contain any logic that justifies a separate file. It receives 10 props, renders one textarea and two switches. This is the kind of extraction that makes code harder to read because you have to jump between files for what was previously a visually scannable block of JSX.

4. **Video-related files are arguably justified:** `StructureVideoSection` (429L) and `StructureVideoPreview` (258L) have genuine complexity with upload state machines, drag-and-drop, and preview rendering. These are reasonable extractions. The `useStructureVideoUpload` hook (247L) encapsulating upload logic is also fine.

**Recommendation:** Merge `PromptSection` back into the parent form -- it is too small to justify the file boundary. Deduplicate `handleSaveFieldAsDefault`. Consider whether `AdvancedSettingsSection` could take a single `settings` + `onChange` prop object instead of 16 individual props to reduce the prop drilling tax. The video-related extractions (`StructureVideoSection`, `StructureVideoPreview`, `useStructureVideoUpload`) are fine.

### 3. MediaGallery GalleryConfig (Wave 5a)

**Rating: GOOD**

**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGallery/types.ts` (GalleryConfig at lines 143-193)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGallery/index.tsx` (config usage at lines 104-119)

**Analysis:**

This is well-done. The config pattern works because:

1. **Multiple consumers (5+):** I found config usage in `ImageGenerationToolPage`, `CharacterAnimatePage`, `EditImagesPage`, `EditVideoPage` (x2), and likely more. Each caller passes a different subset of 2-6 config flags. The `Partial<GalleryConfig>` with defaults pattern means callers only specify what they override.

2. **Clean consumption:** The component merges once at line 108 (`{...DEFAULT_GALLERY_CONFIG, ...configOverrides}`) and then destructures for convenience at line 114. No `config.showDelete` scattered through JSX -- the destructuring makes it identical to the old boolean-props approach for readability.

3. **Grouping is meaningful:** The config separates "which buttons to show" from "which sections to hide" from "UI mode flags." The JSDoc comments on each field make the interface self-documenting.

4. **No over-engineering:** No factory functions, no builders, no generic config system. It is a typed object with defaults. The `Partial<>` type at the call site means callers can still write `config={{ showShare: false }}` which is just as readable as the old `showShare={false}`.

**No changes needed.**

### 4. useDeviceInfo Composite Hook (Wave 2)

**Rating: MILD**

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/use-mobile.tsx` (lines 162-248)

**Analysis:**

The hook has 3 real consumers: `useLayoutMode` (uses `isTabletOrLarger`, `isPortraitMode`, `isTouchDevice`), `SharedGenerationView` (uses `mobileColumns`), and `ShotEditor` (uses `isPhone`, `mobileColumns`). The individual hooks (`useIsMobile`, `useIsTablet`, `useIsTouchDevice`) are also preserved and used separately elsewhere, which is good.

The composite hook adds some value:
- `mobileColumns` is derived state that depends on `isMobile`, `isTablet`, and `orientation`. Without the composite, every consumer would need to compute this themselves.
- `isTabletOrLarger` is the logical inverse of `useIsMobile` but semantically different (it means "wider than phone width" regardless of touch capability). Providing it here avoids the confusing `!isMobile` which would actually be wrong (`isMobile` includes tablets).

**Concerns:**
- The hook subscribes to multiple event listeners (`resize`, media query changes) redundantly with the individual hooks it calls internally. `useIsMobile` already adds a resize listener, `useIsTablet` adds another, and then `useDeviceInfo` adds yet another. A consumer calling `useDeviceInfo` spawns 4+ event listeners.
- `isTouchDevice` at line 198 is in a `useMemo` but depends on `isMobile` which triggers re-renders anyway, so the `useMemo` is doing nothing useful.

**Recommendation:** The hook is justified by `mobileColumns` derived state. The redundant event listeners are a minor inefficiency, not a correctness issue. Leave it, but note the listener duplication if performance profiling ever flags it.

---

### 5. ProjectContext Decomposition (Wave 4)

**Rating: GOOD**

**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx` (130L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectCRUD.ts` (327L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectSelection.ts` (150L)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectDefaults.ts` (66L)

**Total: 673 lines** (vs 653L original -- nearly identical).

**Analysis:**

This is a clean decomposition. The total line count barely changed, which means the extraction added almost no overhead. The three hooks have genuinely distinct concerns:

- `useProjectCRUD` (327L): Database operations -- fetch, create, update, delete. Owns `projects[]` state and all loading flags. This is testable in isolation.
- `useProjectSelection` (150L): Selection persistence -- localStorage fast resume, cross-device sync via server preferences, state transitions on project create/delete. This is the most complex hook and benefits from isolation.
- `useProjectDefaults` (66L): Side effects orchestration -- fetch trigger, cross-device sync timing, prefetching, mobile stall fallback. This is a thin coordinator that wires the other two together.

The thin provider at `ProjectContext.tsx` (130L) composes these cleanly with explicit wiring. The context value is built via `useMemo` with a well-audited dependency array.

**On `useProjectDefaults` specifically:** At 66 lines, it is the thinnest of the three. It could be argued this should be inline in the provider. However, it isolates side-effect wiring (the `useEffect` calls) from data/state logic, which is a clean separation of concerns. The `usePrefetchToolSettings` and `useMobileTimeoutFallback` calls are non-obvious infrastructure hooks that would clutter the provider if inlined.

**No changes needed.**

---

### 6. _shared/billing.ts (Wave 3)

**Rating: GOOD**

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts` (128L)

**Analysis:**

Despite having "only 3 consumers," the extraction is well-justified:

1. **Actual consumer count is 4:** `update-task-status/index.ts` (uses all 4 exports), `complete_task/billing.ts` (re-exports `triggerCostCalculation` + uses `getSubTaskOrchestratorId`), `complete_task/params.ts` (uses `extractOrchestratorRef`), `calculate-task-cost/index.ts` (uses `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` + `buildSubTaskFilter`).

2. **The shared logic is non-trivial and correctness-critical.** `extractOrchestratorRef` handles 4 different param paths where the orchestrator reference can live. `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` has 3 guard checks (null, UUID format, self-reference). Getting any of these wrong across 3 different edge functions would cause billing bugs. DRY is the right call here.

3. **`buildSubTaskFilter` constructs PostgREST query syntax.** Having this in one place means if a 5th param path is added, it is updated once.

4. **`triggerCostCalculation` is a fire-and-forget HTTP call with error handling.** Duplicating this across functions would be pointless.

5. **The file is lean** -- 128 lines, 4 exports, clear JSDoc. No over-abstraction, no class hierarchies, no generic billing framework.

**No changes needed.**

---

### 7. PanesContext createPaneLockSetter Factory (Wave 2)

**Rating: GOOD**

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/PanesContext.tsx` (lines 115-147)

**Analysis:**

The factory pattern at lines 115-143 is a `useCallback` that returns a function. It replaces what would otherwise be 3 nearly-identical setter functions for `gens`, `shots`, and `tasks` lock state. The factory:

1. Handles exclusive locking on mobile/tablet (only one pane locked at a time).
2. Persists to database (skipped for phones).
3. Handles the tasks-specific side effect (syncing `isTasksPaneOpen`).

The derived setters at lines 145-147 use `useMemo` to create stable references:
```typescript
const setIsGenerationsPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('gens'), [createPaneLockSetter]);
const setIsShotsPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('shots'), [createPaneLockSetter]);
const setIsTasksPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('tasks'), [createPaneLockSetter]);
```

This is a well-scoped DRY extraction. The factory is 28 lines, and without it you would have 3 x ~20 lines = 60 lines of nearly-identical code (but with subtle divergence risk -- e.g., forgetting to update the mobile exclusivity logic in one of the three setters). The `lockKey === 'tasks'` guard at line 138 cleanly handles the one pane-specific side effect.

**No changes needed.**

<result>I see the potential issue. When `savePaneLocks` is called on line 131-133, it computes the value to save independently of `setLocks` (line 117-125). The computation is duplicated (`exclusiveLock` computed twice, `{ ...prev, [lockKey]: isLocked }` constructed twice). But the key difference is that the `savePaneLocks` path does NOT use `prev` -- it uses the `isLocked` param directly. For the non-exclusive path (desktop), it calls `savePaneLocks({ [lockKey]: isLocked })` which is a partial update. But `savePaneLocks` is `useUserUIState`'s `update` function which merges the patch. So it correctly saves just the changed field. However, in the exclusive case on mobile, it duplicates the full object `{ shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }`.

This is **safe** because:
1. Both computations use the same inputs (`lockKey`, `isLocked`, `isMobile`, `isTablet`)
2. `savePaneLocks` is a merge-based update (not a full replacement)
3. There's no stale closure issue since the values come from function arguments

## Regression Hunt Report: Waves 1-5a

### 1. ImageEditContext Split (Wave 5a)

**1a. useMemo dependency arrays:** **SAFE**

I compared every field in the `canvasValue`, `formValue`, and `statusValue` objects (lines 79-203 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditContext.tsx`) with their dependency arrays. Every field in each object body appears in the corresponding dependency array. No missing dependencies.

**1b. Backward-compatible useImageEditSafe():** **SAFE**

The function at line 228-233 spreads all three sub-context results (`canvas`, `form`, `status`). The composed type `ImageEditState = ImageEditCanvasState & ImageEditFormState & ImageEditStatusState` (line 53-56) matches. Each sub-context hook returns safe defaults (EMPTY_CANVAS, EMPTY_FORM, EMPTY_STATUS) when no provider exists. No field naming conflicts between the three interfaces.

**1c. InlineEditView + FloatingToolControls:** **BUG (pre-existing, but worsened by Wave 5a)**

This is a confirmed behavioral regression. Here is what happened:

- **Before Wave 5a:** `FloatingToolControls` accepted all edit state as **props** (`editMode`, `brushSize`, `onSetEditMode`, etc.). InlineEditView rendered it without a provider but passed all values as props, and the component used those props. It worked.

- **After Wave 5a:** `FloatingToolControls` was changed to read **everything from `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` context** (line 32-52 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/FloatingToolControls.tsx`). Its TypeScript interface was reduced to just `{ variant: 'tablet' | 'mobile' }` (line 14-16). InlineEditView still passes all the old props (lines 757-775 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/edit-images/components/InlineEditView.tsx`), but they are silently ignored because the component no longer reads them.

- **Runtime behavior:** InlineEditView has no `ImageEditProvider` wrapping `FloatingToolControls`. So `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` returns `EMPTY_CANVAS` -- all no-op functions, `editMode: null`, `brushSize: 20`, etc. The floating tool controls panel renders visually but is completely non-functional: clicking mode buttons does nothing, brush size changes do nothing, undo/clear do nothing.

- **Why no TS error:** TypeScript allows excess JSX props in this configuration (the project has `strictNullChecks: false` and no `strict: true`).

- **Impact:** The entire Edit Images tool's inline editor floating tool bar is broken. Users cannot switch edit modes or adjust brush settings from the floating panel.

---

### 2. SegmentSettingsForm Extraction (Wave 5a)

**2a. AdvancedSettingsSection props:** **SAFE**

The `AdvancedSettingsSection` component (lines 28-59 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/AdvancedSettingsSection.tsx`) defines its own interface and receives all needed props from the parent `SegmentSettingsForm` at lines 292-314 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/SegmentSettingsForm.tsx`. All props are passed through correctly.

**2b. handleSaveFieldAsDefault duplication:** **SAFE**

Both `SegmentSettingsForm` (line 144-156) and `AdvancedSettingsSection` (line 186-198) have their own `handleSaveFieldAsDefault` implementations. They are functionally identical: both call `onSaveFieldAsDefault`, set a local `savingField` state, and on success call `onChange({ [field]: undefined })`. The parent also passes its version to `PromptSection` as `handleSaveFieldAsDefault` prop (line 287). `AdvancedSettingsSection` receives the raw `onSaveFieldAsDefault` callback (line 301) and wraps it in its own local handler. Each extracted component manages its own `savingField` loading state, which is correct since they're independent UI elements.

The `PromptSection` receives both `onSaveFieldAsDefault` (the raw callback, for conditional rendering) and `handleSaveFieldAsDefault` (the parent's wrapped version with savingField tracking). This is slightly redundant -- PromptSection uses the parent's `handleSaveFieldAsDefault` and `savingField` rather than managing its own -- but it works correctly.

### 4. PanesContext savePaneLocks (Wave 4 fix)

**4a. Correct newLocks computation:** **SAFE**

The `createPaneLockSetter` at lines 115-143 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/PanesContext.tsx` computes the locks value twice: once inside `setLocks(prev => ...)` for state, once outside for persistence. Both use the same deterministic inputs (`lockKey`, `isLocked`, `isMobile`, `isTablet`) with no dependency on previous state for the persistence call. The `savePaneLocks` path correctly:
- For exclusive mode (mobile/tablet): saves the full object `{ shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }`.
- For non-exclusive mode (desktop): saves a partial `{ [lockKey]: isLocked }` which is merged by `useUserUIState`'s debounced update.

**4b. Race condition between setLocks and savePaneLocks:** **SAFE**

There is no race condition. `savePaneLocks` is `useUserUIState`'s `update` function which immediately updates local state and debounces the DB write. `setLocks` updates `PanesContext`'s own local state. These are independent state atoms. The UI reads from `locks` (PanesContext local state), not from `useUserUIState`'s value. The `useUserUIState` value is only used for initial hydration (line 57: `const [locks, setLocks] = useState(paneLocks)` and the hydration effect at line 72).

---

### 5. ProjectContext Decomposition (Wave 4)

**5a. useProjectCRUD callbacks:** **RISKY (minor)**

`deleteProject` (lines 300-304 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectCRUD.ts`) calls `onProjectDeleted(updated)` **inside** `setProjects`'s updater function. This is a side effect inside a React state updater, which React explicitly warns against. The callback `onProjectDeleted` (which is `useProjectSelection.handleProjectDeleted`) calls `setSelectedProjectIdState(nextProjectId)` and `updateUserSettings(...)`. In React 18's batching model, these state updates will be batched with the `setProjects` update, so it works in practice. But it's an anti-pattern: if React ever changes updater function semantics (e.g., calling them multiple times in concurrent mode), this could fire the callback multiple times.

`fetchProjects` and `addNewProject` correctly call their callbacks (`onProjectsLoaded`, `onProjectCreated`) outside of state updaters, which is the right pattern.

**5b. selectedProjectId persistence (useProjectSelection):** **RISKY (minor)**

`handleProjectDeleted` at lines 129-138 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProjectSelection.ts` calls `setSelectedProjectIdState(nextProjectId)` directly but does NOT update `localStorage`. Compare with `handleSetSelectedProjectId` (lines 86-106) which always writes to localStorage. So after deleting a project, the fast-resume localStorage value still contains the deleted project's ID. On next page load, the app will try to select the deleted project from localStorage, fail the `projectIds.has(preferredId)` check in `determineProjectIdToSelect`, and fall back to `lastOpenedId` or `projects[0]`. This is functionally correct but suboptimal -- there's a brief period where the stale ID is in localStorage, and `updateUserSettings` is called but localStorage lags behind. Not a crash bug, but a minor consistency issue.

---

### 6. window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__ DEV gate removal (Wave 4)

**Verdict: SAFE**

At lines 68-75 of `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx`:

```typescript
useEffect(() => {
    if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
      window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__ = {
        selectedProjectId: selection.selectedProjectId,
        projects: crud.projects,
      };
    }
  }, [selection.selectedProjectId, crud.projects]);
```

The code correctly has no DEV gate. The comment at lines 65-67 explicitly documents this is a structural global (not debug-only), consumed by `useProjectGenerations`, `useTasks`, and `ImageGenerationToolPage` as a fallback. The `typeof window !== 'undefined'` guard handles SSR correctly. No regression here.

---

## Summary

| Area | Verdict | Details |
|------|---------|---------|
| 1a. ImageEditContext useMemo deps | **SAFE** | All dependency arrays are complete |
| 1b. useImageEditSafe() backward compat | **SAFE** | Spread of 3 sub-contexts works correctly |
| 1c. InlineEditView + FloatingToolControls | **BUG** | FloatingToolControls changed from props to context-only; InlineEditView has no provider, so the floating panel is completely non-functional (all controls use empty defaults) |
| 2a. AdvancedSettingsSection props | **SAFE** | All props passed through correctly |
| 2b. handleSaveFieldAsDefault duplication | **SAFE** | Independent instances with correct behavior |
| 3. MediaGallery config migration | **SAFE** | All 8 callers migrated correctly; defaults match original behavior |
| 4a. PanesContext savePaneLocks computation | **SAFE** | Deterministic duplicate computation, no stale state |
| 4b. PanesContext race condition | **SAFE** | Independent state atoms, no race |
| 5a. useProjectCRUD callbacks | **RISKY** | `onProjectDeleted` called inside `setProjects` updater (side effect in state updater) |
| 5b. selectedProjectId localStorage | **RISKY** | `handleProjectDeleted` skips localStorage update, leaving stale entry |
| 6. window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__ | **SAFE** | DEV gate correctly absent; structural global documented |

**One definite BUG** (FloatingToolControls in InlineEditView) and **two minor RISKY items** (side effect in state updater, stale localStorage on delete). The BUG is the highest priority -- it breaks the Edit Images inline editor's floating toolbar entirely.</result>

<result>Zero type errors. Here is a summary of what was done:

---

## Summary

### Problem
`ImageLightbox.tsx` was 1022 lines, handling lightbox display, image editing orchestration (inpainting, magic edit, reposition, img2img), context value construction, and panel rendering -- all in a single component.

### Solution
Extracted a `useImageEditOrchestrator` hook that encapsulates all edit-mode hook composition and the `ImageEditState` context value construction.

### Files Changed

**New file: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/hooks/useImageEditOrchestrator.ts`** (535 lines)

This hook composes:
- `useInpainting` -- canvas drawing, mask creation, inpaint task generation
- `useEditSettingsSync` -- bidirectional sync between persisted settings and active edit state
- `useMagicEditMode` -- magic edit mode state, auto-enter, unified generate handler
- `useRepositionMode` -- transform state, drag interaction, reposition task/variant creation
- `useImg2ImgMode` -- img2img generation with strength/prompt controls

It also builds the complete `ImageEditState` context value (the large `useMemo` that constructs the object passed to `ImageEditProvider`).

The hook takes a well-defined props interface and returns:
- `imageEditValue` for the context provider
- Mode flags (`isInpaintMode`, `isMagicEditMode`, `isSpecialEditMode`, `editMode`)
- Mode entry/exit handlers
- Generation handlers for the edit panel
- The img2img LoRA manager

**Modified: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/ImageLightbox.tsx`** (658 lines, down from 1022)

The component now follows a clear linear flow:
1. Core setup (project, panes, refs, state)
2. Image dimensions + upscale hook
3. Edit settings persistence + LoRA management
4. Shared lightbox state (with local proxy pattern for edit mode flags)
5. **`useImageEditOrchestrator`** -- single call replaces ~365 lines of hook composition
6. Supporting hooks (adjusted task details, panel mode restore, variant badges)
7. Context value construction
8. Handler definitions + layout decisions
9. Panel content + layout props
10. Render

**Modified: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/hooks/index.ts`** (1 line added)

Added the `useImageEditOrchestrator` export.

### Circular Dependency Handling

The original code had a circular dependency: `useSharedLightboxState` needs edit mode flags for layout calculations, but the edit hooks need variant data from shared state. The original code solved this with local state proxies that update one frame later via `useEffect`. The refactored code preserves this exact pattern:

1. `isInpaintModeLocal` / `isMagicEditModeLocal` state proxies live in ImageLightbox
2. They're passed to `useSharedLightboxState`
3. `useImageEditOrchestrator` runs after shared state, receiving real variant data
4. A `useEffect` syncs the orchestrator's real mode values back to the proxies

### What Was NOT Extracted

- **Props interface** (46 lines) -- irreducible, defines the component's API
- **Controls panel content** (`useMemo` with `EditModePanel`/`InfoPanel`) -- extracting would require 20+ props, adding complexity
- **Layout props construction** -- already delegated to `useLightboxWorkflowProps` hook
- **Core setup** (project, panes, upscale, edit settings, LoRA) -- loosely coupled, not a single cohesive concern

### Verification
`npx tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors. No files outside `src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/` were touched.</result>

<result>Zero errors. The refactoring is complete. Here is a summary of what was done:

---

## Summary

### Problem
`TimelineContainer.tsx` was 988 lines with interaction logic, trailing endpoint management, pair settings routing, and JSX rendering all mixed together.

### Approach
After thorough analysis, I identified that the component's size comes from two sources:
1. **Interaction/state logic** (~260 lines) -- callbacks, effects, derived state
2. **JSX rendering** (~530 lines) -- inherent UI complexity rendering segments, endpoints, strips, controls

Per the task rules, I focused on extracting pure interaction/state logic (not rendering logic). The key constraint was a **data dependency ordering**: the `useTimelineOrchestrator` hook needs `hasExistingTrailingVideo` (derived from `framePositions`), while downstream logic needs `currentPositions` (from the orchestrator). This required splitting trailing endpoint logic into pre-orchestrator and post-orchestrator phases.

### Files Modified/Created

**Modified:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/Timeline/TimelineContainer/TimelineContainer.tsx` (988 -> 795 lines)

**Created:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/Timeline/hooks/useTrailingEndpoint.ts` (157 lines) -- Manages trailing video URL state, last-image-change tracking, image-only positions derivation, and video frame extraction
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/Timeline/hooks/usePairSettingsHandler.ts` (119 lines) -- Handles the complex 3-case pair click routing logic (single-image, multi-image trailing, regular pairs)

### What Each Hook Contains

**`useTrailingEndpoint`** (157 lines):
- `trailingVideoUrl` state (computed + callback fallback)
- `imagePositions` derived from `currentPositions` (excluding trailing endpoint key)
- Last image ID tracking effect (clears stale trailing video URLs)
- `handleExtractFinalFrame` -- creates a video element, seeks to final frame, captures to canvas, converts to file, and drops at target position

**`usePairSettingsHandler`** (119 lines):
- `handleOpenPairSettings` callback with 3 code paths:
  - Single-image mode (trailing segment is only segment)
  - Multi-image trailing segment (pairIndex after regular pairs)
  - Regular pair segments

### What Stayed in TimelineContainer

- Pre-orchestrator trailing values (29 lines) -- `trailingEndFrame`, `handleTrailingEndFrameChange`, `hasExistingTrailingVideo` computation -- must run before orchestrator due to data dependency
- Orchestrator hook call and destructuring (82 lines)
- Derived pending pair data (14 lines)
- Simple handlers: `handleReset`, `handleDeleteSelected`, `handleNewShotFromSelection` (21 lines)
- JSX rendering (~519 lines) -- inherent to the component's UI complexity

### Verification
`npx tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors. All external APIs (exports, props, context shapes) remain identical. No files outside `src/tools/travel-between-images/components/Timeline/` were touched.</result>

Each wave should be followed by a sense check evaluation, committed, then proceed to the next wave.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **React Context decomposition**: Splitting monolithic contexts into sub-contexts with `useMemo` slicing for re-render optimization (ImageEditContext → 3 sub-contexts)
   - **GalleryConfig pattern**: Grouping boolean flag props into `Partial<GalleryConfig>` with `DEFAULT_GALLERY_CONFIG`
   - **Hook composition**: Splitting large hooks into sub-hooks with thin composition layers (useGenerationActions → 3 sub-hooks, useAutoSaveSettings → useDebouncedSettingsSave)
   - **ImageEditProvider wrapping**: The pre-existing InlineEditView bug where FloatingToolControls and MediaDisplayWithCanvas read from context defaults instead of the props being passed
   - **Pure React state updaters**: Side effects inside `setProjects` updater function violate React's purity contract; must be moved outside
   - **ES module strict mode**: Bare `supabase` without import throws ReferenceError in strict mode even if `window.supabase` exists
   - **TypeScript loose mode**: `strict: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `strictNullChecks: false` in tsconfig means excess JSX props are silently ignored
   - **Prompt race-condition protection**: `useEditSettingsSync` detects when inpainting hook resets prompt to empty during re-initialization and restores from persistence
   - **Parallel sub-agent execution**: 3-5 agents per wave on independent files, with dependency tracking between waves
   - **FK migration deploy ordering**: PostgREST `!tasks_task_type_fkey` join hint requires migration to be deployed before the edge function

       onProjectDeleted(updated);
       return updated;

     // AFTER (pure updater):
     const updated = projects.filter(p => p.id !== projectId);
     setProjects(updated);
     onProjectDeleted(updated);
     ```

       // Added: localStorage write for fast resume
       try {
         if (nextProjectId) {
           localStorage.setItem('lastSelectedProjectId', nextProjectId);
         } else {
           localStorage.removeItem('lastSelectedProjectId');
         }
       } catch { /* localStorage unavailable */ }
       // ... existing updateUserSettings calls ...
     }, [updateUserSettings]);
     ```

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **ImageLightbox missing destructured props** (found by sense-check agent ac670b2):
     - `positionedInSelectedShot` and `associatedWithoutPositionInSelectedShot` were in the props interface but not destructured from `props`, causing bare identifier ReferenceError
     - Fixed by adding them to the destructuring block
   
   - **useDropActions bare supabase** (found by sense-check agent ac670b2):
     - `supabase` used without import on lines 122 and 291; ES modules run in strict mode so bare `supabase` throws ReferenceError even though `window.supabase` exists
     - Fixed by adding `import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client'`
   
   - **useProjectCRUD side effect in state updater** (found by sense-check agent a071cbb):
     - `onProjectDeleted(updated)` called inside `setProjects` updater function violates React's purity contract
     - Fixed by moving outside: compute `updated` from `projects` directly, then call `setProjects(updated)` and `onProjectDeleted(updated)` separately
     - Added `projects` to useCallback dependency array
   
   - **useProjectSelection localStorage gap** (found by sense-check agent aef8f57):
     - `handleProjectDeleted` used `setSelectedProjectIdState` directly instead of `handleSetSelectedProjectId`, skipping localStorage write
     - Fixed by adding explicit localStorage.setItem/removeItem call
   
   - **InlineEditView context bug** (pre-existing, found during Wave 5a review):
     - FloatingToolControls and MediaDisplayWithCanvas both read from ImageEditCanvasContext but InlineEditView had no provider
     - Both components got EMPTY_CANVAS defaults; 20+ props passed to them were silently ignored
     - Fixed by: adding missing canvas fields to imageEditValue, importing ImageEditProvider, wrapping InlineEditCanvas in provider, removing ghost props
   
   - **Over-engineering review corrections**:
     - SegmentSettingsForm "OVER" rating was debunked — pre-Wave5a directory total was 1894 lines (not 1044), so increase was only 15%
     - InlineEditView/FloatingToolControls regression claim was debunked — FloatingToolControls was already context-only before Wave 5a

5. Problem Solving:
   - Discovered that `tsconfig.app.json` has `strict: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `strictNullChecks: false` — explaining why TypeScript doesn't catch excess JSX props or bare variable references
   - Traced the InlineEditView bug through both FloatingToolControls AND MediaDisplayWithCanvas, discovering both components were affected
   - Verified that `imageEditValue` was missing ~20 canvas fields by cross-referencing `ImageEditCanvasState` interface with the useMemo construction
   - Verified wave 5b sense check findings by reading actual git diffs to distinguish pre-existing issues from introduced regressions
   - The `window.__PROJECT_CONTEXT__` DEV gate issue was confirmed FIXED in Wave 4 (write is unconditional)
   - The PanesContext side effect issue was confirmed FIXED in Wave 2 (savePaneLocks moved outside updater)

<result>Everything is clean. The migration is complete. Here is a summary of the changes made.

---

## Summary of changes

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/ai-prompt/index.ts`

### What was changed

**1. Added import for the shared auth helper (line 7):**
```typescript
import { authenticateRequest } from "../_shared/auth.ts";
```

**2. Replaced manual JWT extraction (old lines 37-59) with the shared `authenticateRequest()` pattern (new lines 37-46):**

**Before (manual auth):**
```typescript
const supabaseUrl = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL");
const serviceKey = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY");
const anonKey = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY");

if (!supabaseUrl || !serviceKey || !anonKey) {
  console.error("[ai-prompt] Missing required environment variables");
  return jsonResponse({ error: "Server configuration error" }, 500);
}

const authHeader = req.headers.get("Authorization");
if (!authHeader?.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
  return jsonResponse({ error: "Missing or invalid Authorization header" }, 401);
}

const [REDACTED]
const supabaseAdmin = createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey);

const { data: { user }, error: authError } = await supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token);
if (authError || !user) {
  console.error("[ai-prompt] Invalid authentication token:", authError?.message);
  return jsonResponse({ error: "Invalid authentication token" }, 401);
}
```

**After (shared helper):**
```typescript
const supabaseAdmin = createClient(
  Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL") ?? "",
  Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") ?? ""
);

const auth = await authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[AI-PROMPT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true });
if (!auth.success || !auth.userId) {
  return jsonResponse({ error: auth.error || "Authentication failed" }, auth.statusCode || 401);
}
```

**3. Updated rate limit call to use `auth.userId` instead of `user.id` (line 52):**
```typescript
// Before:
user.id,
// After:
auth.userId,
```

### What was NOT changed

All non-auth logic remains untouched: the `jsonResponse` helper, Groq/OpenAI client setup, JSON body parsing, all four task handlers (`generate_prompts`, `edit_prompt`, `generate_summary`, `enhance_segment_prompt`), and error handling.

### Behavioral improvements

- **Still returns 401** for unauthenticated requests (via `auth.statusCode || 401`)
- **Rate limiting still works** using `auth.userId`
- **Now supports PATs** in addition to JWTs (via `allowJwtUserAuth: true` + the shared helper's PAT lookup), matching the other migrated edge functions
- **Removed dependency on `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`** which was fetched but only used indirectly through `getUser()` -- the shared helper doesn't need it
- **Removed `getUser()` call** which was an extra network round-trip to Supabase Auth; the shared helper decodes the JWT payload directly for user ID extraction</result>

<result>`tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly with no errors.

Here is a summary of all changes made to `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/index.ts`:

## Changes made

### 1. Removed `@ts-nocheck` (line 2)
The `// @ts-nocheck` directive was removed. This was the last remaining instance in the entire codebase (the only other hits are in task planning markdown files, not source code).

### 2. Added three minimal interfaces (lines 8-31)
Three interfaces were added to type the Stripe webhook payloads:

- **`StripeEvent`** -- Models the shape of any Stripe webhook event: `{ type: string, data: { object: Record<string, unknown> } }`. Using `Record<string, unknown>` for `data.object` since Stripe objects vary by event type and we don't import the Stripe SDK.

- **`CheckoutSessionMetadata`** -- Models the metadata fields set during checkout creation: `userId`, `amount`, `autoTopupEnabled`, `autoTopupAmount`, `autoTopupThreshold`. All string fields (Stripe metadata values are always strings).

- **`PaymentIntentMetadata`** -- Models the metadata for auto-top-up payment intents: `autoTopup`, `userId`, `originalBalance`, `topupAmount`.

### 3. Typed `jsonResponse` parameter (line 34)
Changed `body: any` to `body: Record<string, unknown>`. Every call site passes an object literal with string keys, so this is the correct narrowing.

### 4. Added type assertion for `JSON.parse` (line 119)
`JSON.parse(body)` is now `JSON.parse(body) as StripeEvent`. This gives the parsed event object a known shape.

### 5. Typed metadata extractions with assertions (lines 129, 252, 328)
- `session.metadata` cast via `(session.metadata ?? {}) as CheckoutSessionMetadata`
- `paymentIntent.metadata` cast via `(paymentIntent.metadata ?? {}) as PaymentIntentMetadata`
- `failedPaymentIntent.metadata` cast via `(failedPaymentIntent.metadata ?? {}) as PaymentIntentMetadata`

### 6. Added `as` assertions for `Record<string, unknown>` property access
Where values from `event.data.object` (typed as `Record<string, unknown>`) are used in contexts that need specific types:
- `session.payment_intent as string | undefined` (line 148)
- `session.customer as string` (line 175)
- `session.id as string` (lines 201, 232, 243)
- `session.amount_total as number` (lines 141, 241)
- `paymentIntent.id as string` (lines 275, 309, 316)
- `failedPaymentIntent.id as string` (line 337)
- `failedPaymentIntent.last_payment_error as Record<string, unknown> | undefined` (line 334)
- `lastPaymentError?.code as string | undefined` (line 343)

### 7. Typed all catch clauses with `unknown` (lines 179, 204, 278, 378)
All `catch (error)` and `catch (checkErr)` clauses now use `: unknown`. Error message access was changed from `error.message` to the safe pattern `error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)`. The `checkErr` variables already used `String(checkErr)` so those just needed the `: unknown` annotation.

### What did NOT change
- Zero logic changes -- all control flow, branching, and data operations are identical
- No new files created
- No other files touched
- All existing comments and behavior preserved</result>

<result>All changes are correct. Here is a summary of what was done:

---

## Summary

### Changes Made

**1. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts`**
- Added import of `checkRateLimit`, `rateLimitResponse`, and `RATE_LIMITS` from `../_shared/rateLimit.ts`
- Added rate limiting after authentication (step 4b), but **only for non-service-role callers**. Workers that call `complete_task` use the service-role key and are not rate limited, since they are internal and self-regulating. User-facing callers (JWT/PAT auth) are rate limited using `RATE_LIMITS.userAction` (60 requests per minute per user).

**2. `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/delete-project/index.ts`**
- Added import of `checkRateLimit` and `rateLimitResponse` from `../_shared/rateLimit.ts`
- Added rate limiting after authentication with a custom config of **10 requests per minute per user** -- a conservative limit appropriate for this destructive operation that triggers cascading deletes with an extended timeout.

**3. `calculate-task-cost` -- intentionally NOT modified**
- This function is **service-role only** (line 148 explicitly checks `authHeader.slice(7) !== serviceKey` and rejects non-service-role callers with 401). It is only called internally by other edge functions/workers (e.g., `complete_task` calls it via `triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask`). Rate limiting internal service-to-service calls would be counterproductive -- it could cause billing failures for legitimate task completions.

### Design Decisions

- **Same pattern as `ai-prompt`**: Both new rate limiters use the shared `checkRateLimit` / `rateLimitResponse` utilities from `_shared/rateLimit.ts`, keying on `user.id` (not IP).
- **Fail-open behavior preserved**: The shared `checkRateLimit` function already handles errors by failing open (allowing the request through if the rate limit RPC fails), so no additional error handling was needed.
- **Logger integration**: Both functions log a warning via `SystemLogger` when rate limits are exceeded, then flush before returning the 429 response.
- **No non-rate-limiting logic changed**: All existing functionality remains untouched.</result>

<result>Now I have all the information needed for a thorough review. Let me compile my findings.

---

## Review Report: Wave 5d Refactoring

### TypeScript Check

`npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly -- no type errors.

---

### Findings

**BUG | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/usePromptManagement.ts`:400-434 | Prompt dedup effect can infinite-loop when prompts are shot-backed**

The dedup effect's dependency array is `[prompts, setPrompts, promptIdCounter]`. The `prompts` value is a computed `useMemo` that returns a new array reference from `shotPromptSettings.settings.prompts`. When `hadDuplicates` is true, the effect calls `setPrompts(dedupedPrompts)` which calls `shotPromptSettings.updateField('prompts', updatedPrompts)`. This triggers a new settings save, which when it resolves produces a new `settings.prompts` reference, which makes `prompts` useMemo recompute with a new reference, which re-triggers the effect. 

In the **original** code (in `useImageGenForm.ts`), the effect had the same dependency on `[prompts, setPrompts]`, so this risk existed before too. However, the original effect did NOT have `promptIdCounter` in its dep array (it used it from closure). Adding `promptIdCounter` is a no-op since it's a ref and never changes identity, so it doesn't make things worse. This is a pre-existing latent risk, not a regression.

**Verdict: Pre-existing, not a regression. MILD.**

---

**MILD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useShotManagement.ts`:108 | Added `setAssociatedShotId` to dep array of initialShotId effect**

The original effect at line 419 of the old `useImageGenForm.ts` had deps `[ready, initialShotId, shots, associatedShotId]` -- notably missing `setAssociatedShotId`. The extracted version adds `setAssociatedShotId` to the array. Since `setAssociatedShotId` comes from `useState` and has a stable identity, this is a correctness improvement (satisfying exhaustive-deps) with no behavioral change.

**Verdict: GOOD - fixes a missing dependency.**

---

**GOOD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useShotManagement.ts`:122-136 | handleShotChange upgraded from plain function to useCallback**

The original `handleShotChange` was a plain (non-memoized) function defined inline in the hook body. The extraction wraps it in `useCallback` with proper dependencies. This is a minor performance improvement and more correct for a function passed as a callback prop.

---

**GOOD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/ai-prompt/index.ts` | Auth migration is correct**

The original used `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)` for JWT verification. The new code uses `authenticateRequest(req, supabaseAdmin, "[AI-PROMPT]", { allowJwtUserAuth: true })`. The `authenticateRequest` helper with `allowJwtUserAuth: true` decodes the JWT and extracts `payload.sub` as the user ID, which is equivalent to what `auth.getUser()` returned. Rate limiting behavior is preserved identically -- same `checkRateLimit` call with same `RATE_LIMITS.expensive` config and `auth.userId` (previously `user.id`).

One minor difference: the original rejected service-role JWTs implicitly (because `getUser` wouldn't return a user for a service key). The new `authenticateRequest` handles this explicitly by rejecting service_role/supabase_admin JWT roles and only accepting direct service key match. This is actually better security behavior. The function also now accepts PATs (Personal Access Tokens) in addition to JWTs, which is a feature improvement consistent with the rest of the edge functions.

---

**GOOD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts`:181-195 | Rate limit correctly skips service-role workers**

The guard `if (!isServiceRole && callerId)` ensures workers using the service-role key bypass rate limiting. This is exactly correct -- workers complete tasks at high volume and should not be rate-limited. The `RATE_LIMITS.userAction` (60/min) is appropriate for user-initiated completions (e.g., local generation uploads).

---

**GOOD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/delete-project/index.ts`:36-48 | Rate limit addition is clean**

Custom inline limit of `{ maxRequests: 10, windowSeconds: 60 }` is appropriate for a destructive operation. The `identifierType: 'user' as const` matches the pattern. Logger flush is called before returning the 429.

---

**MILD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/usePromptManagement.ts`:53 | promptIdCounter is optional but always passed**

The `promptIdCounter` prop is typed as `React.MutableRefObject<number> | undefined` (optional). In practice it is always passed from `useImageGenForm`. The `if (!promptIdCounter) return` early-exit in the dedup effect means if someone ever calls `usePromptManagement` without it, the dedup won't run and duplicate IDs can cause the "multiple prompts update together" bug. Making it required would be safer.

---

**MILD | `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useFormContextBuilder.ts`:27-46 | Dynamic import types in interface**

The `Pick<ReturnType<typeof import('./usePromptManagement').usePromptManagement>, ...>` pattern is clever and ensures the interface stays in sync with the hook return type. However, it's unusual and could confuse developers. It works correctly (verified by `tsc`), so this is a style observation only.
